\section{Phase 7 - Parts 601-700}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-phase-7---parts-601-700}

Six pasted source excerpts (P7-A through P7-F, identifiers for these attachments only) contain 84 standalone lessons: 601--634 and 651--700. Parts 635--650 have no supplied lesson. The continuous volume reconstructs the affine prerequisite bridge without inventing those missing canonical titles. The table is a source ledger, not the teaching order.

At the Phase 7 ledger snapshot, numbered topics with developed source/reconstruction entries totaled 344: Parts1--160,501--634,651--700. Of those,343 have supplied numbered lessons; Part542 remains explicitly reconstructed. The16 missing Phase7 sources are recorded as gaps rather than counted as received lessons. Parts161--500 and all parts after 700 were unreceived at that Phase 7 snapshot; Phase 8 coverage is recorded below. Phase7 attachment boundaries are not silently assigned integrated-chunk identifiers.

\section{Phase 6 - Parts 501-600}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-phase-6---parts-501-600}

The user supplied the Phase 6 group identified as M031--M036 in nineteen pasted documents, including a supplemental panel analogy. Explicit lesson headings cover 99 distinct parts: 501--541 and 543--600. Part 542 is referenced as the next cross-product definition but has no standalone lesson; its mechanism is explicitly reconstructed from adjacent material in the new volume. The narrative therefore develops all 100 phase topics without falsely claiming 100 standalone source lessons.

Before Phase 7, coverage was Parts 1--160 and 501--600: 260 developed entries, including the Part 542 reconstruction. The Phase 7 snapshot extended developed numbered coverage to 344 entries and recorded its 16 missing source lessons separately. The explicitly identified chunk groups remain M001--M010 and user-labeled M031--M036; the six new Phase 7 excerpts are not assigned unverified integrated-chunk identifiers. XR01/Modules 1--19 remain separate.

501 & SCALARS AS ELEMENTS OF A NUMBER FIELD --- Field laws make nonzero division and vector scaling meaningful; a floating type is only an approximation. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-52051ad550fe}{Scalars fields and real-number models (02-Foundations-II, appendix-52051ad550fe)} \\
502 & REAL NUMBERS AND THE PROPERTIES GRAPHICS RELIES ON --- Order, intervals and completeness justify real geometry; bounded machine values are not the complete number line. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-52051ad550fe}{Scalars fields and real-number models (02-Foundations-II, appendix-52051ad550fe)} \\
503 & COMPLEX NUMBERS AND TWO-DIMENSIONAL ROTATION INTUITION --- Unit complex multiplication rotates a plane; conjugation reverses it, and magnitude drift adds unwanted scale. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f77eadcc4a93}{Complex numbers and planar rotations (02-Foundations-II, appendix-f77eadcc4a93)} \\
504 & VECTORS AS GEOMETRIC DISPLACEMENTS --- Free displacement survives translation of its starting point; location and movement are different roles. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
505 & VECTORS AS COORDINATE TUPLES --- An ordered tuple gives components, but neither its basis nor physical role follows from storage shape. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
506 & THE DISTINCTION BETWEEN A VECTOR AND ITS COORDINATES --- One physical vector has different coordinates in different bases; distinguish passive description from active motion. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
507 & VECTOR SPACES AND THEIR AXIOMS --- Closure and vector-space laws enable consistent combinations; unit directions are a constrained non-example. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-173b52c063ae}{Vector spaces subspaces and span (02-Foundations-II, appendix-173b52c063ae)} \\
508 & SUBSPACES --- A nonempty subset closed under arbitrary two-vector combinations is a subspace; shifted planes need affine language. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-173b52c063ae}{Vector spaces subspaces and span (02-Foundations-II, appendix-173b52c063ae)} \\
509 & LINEAR COMBINATIONS --- Arbitrary coefficients generate movements, not necessarily averages; negative weights are valid. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-173b52c063ae}{Vector spaces subspaces and span (02-Foundations-II, appendix-173b52c063ae)} \\
510 & SPAN --- The span is the smallest subspace containing generators; redundant inputs do not enlarge it. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-173b52c063ae}{Vector spaces subspaces and span (02-Foundations-II, appendix-173b52c063ae)} \\
511 & LINEAR INDEPENDENCE --- A zero combination must have only zero coefficients for independence; near dependence is a numerical question. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
512 & BASIS VECTORS --- Spanning ensures reachability and independence ensures uniqueness; both are necessary for a basis. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
513 & DIMENSION OF A VECTOR SPACE --- Dimension counts basis elements over the selected field; a plane stored in three components still has dimension two. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
514 & COORDINATE REPRESENTATION RELATIVE TO A BASIS --- Coordinates weight an ordered basis; reconstruct the physical arrow before interpreting the tuple. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
515 & CHANGING BASIS CONCEPTUALLY --- Changing coordinates solves the new basis relation; near-parallel basis axes amplify coefficient uncertainty. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
516 & STANDARD BASIS IN \(\mathbb R^2\) --- The two standard unit axes reconstruct every planar coordinate; visual up/right conventions remain separate. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
517 & STANDARD BASIS IN \(\mathbb R^3\) --- Three standard unit axes reconstruct spatial coordinates; no universal camera forward direction follows. \hyperref[appendix-ce0341f7ea0d]{Basis coordinates and dimension} \\
518 & VECTOR ADDITION FROM THE PARALLELOGRAM RULE --- Head-to-tail and parallelogram movement yield componentwise addition in a common basis. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
519 & SCALAR MULTIPLICATION OF VECTORS --- Scalar scaling stretches and may reverse direction; velocity times elapsed time yields displacement with correct units. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
520 & VECTOR SUBTRACTION AS DISPLACEMENT DIFFERENCE --- Subtraction adds the opposite vector; ordered point difference produces the displacement from one location to another. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
521 & AFFINE POINTS VERSUS FREE VECTORS --- Affine points have no intrinsic zero; point differences and translations are valid without choosing an origin. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
522 & POSITION VECTORS AND WHY POINTS ARE NOT MATHEMATICALLY VECTORS --- A position vector is point minus chosen origin; changing the origin cancels only in appropriate differences. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
523 & DIRECTION VECTORS --- Direction answers which way and must be nonzero; translation does not affect it. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
524 & DISPLACEMENT VECTORS --- Displacement records change in position and may validly be zero; units and frame must match. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
525 & UNIT VECTORS --- Unit vectors retain direction but remove magnitude; the zero vector cannot satisfy this promise. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0659864c0aa3}{Vectors coordinates and affine points (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0659864c0aa3)} \\
526 & EUCLIDEAN VECTOR MAGNITUDE --- Pythagoras yields Euclidean length only in orthonormal coordinates; a slanted basis needs its metric. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
527 & SQUARED MAGNITUDE AND WHY GRAPHICS OFTEN AVOIDS SQUARE ROOTS --- Squared length avoids some square roots in comparisons but is neither a norm nor a metric. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
528 & NORMALIZING A VECTOR --- Divide a nonzero vector by its length to normalize; this operation is nonlinear. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f744e41b95b4}{Unit directions and safe normalization (02-Foundations-II, appendix-f744e41b95b4)} \\
529 & ZERO-VECTOR NORMALIZATION AND ROBUST HANDLING --- Reject zero/non-finite inputs and scale before squaring; application noise thresholds are a separate policy. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f744e41b95b4}{Unit directions and safe normalization (02-Foundations-II, appendix-f744e41b95b4)} \\
530 & L1 NORM --- The first norm sums absolute coordinates; its unit ball is a coordinate-dependent diamond. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
531 & L2 NORM --- The second norm is Euclidean; Cauchy--Schwarz proves its triangle inequality. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
532 & L-INFINITY NORM --- The infinity norm is the maximum absolute component; its unit ball is a coordinate-dependent box. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
533 & DISTANCE INDUCED BY A NORM --- A norm induces a metric by measuring point difference; different norms can choose different nearest points. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-49933872bb51}{Norms lengths and induced distances (02-Foundations-II, appendix-49933872bb51)} \\
534 & DOT PRODUCT ALGEBRAIC DEFINITION --- The real-coordinate dot sums component products under an orthonormal metric assumption. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-8bc4e8fc547c}{Dot products and orthogonality (02-Foundations-II, appendix-8bc4e8fc547c)} \\
535 & DOT PRODUCT GEOMETRIC INTERPRETATION --- Dot measures magnitude-weighted alignment; it equals cosine only for unit inputs. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-8bc4e8fc547c}{Dot products and orthogonality (02-Foundations-II, appendix-8bc4e8fc547c)} \\
536 & DERIVING THE COSINE-ANGLE RELATION FOR THE DOT PRODUCT --- Compare an expanded squared difference with the cosine rule to derive the angle relation. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-8bc4e8fc547c}{Dot products and orthogonality (02-Foundations-II, appendix-8bc4e8fc547c)} \\
537 & ORTHOGONAL VECTORS --- Orthogonality is zero inner product; zero has no angle, and numerical tests need scale-aware criteria. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-8bc4e8fc547c}{Dot products and orthogonality (02-Foundations-II, appendix-8bc4e8fc547c)} \\
538 & VECTOR PROJECTION ONTO ANOTHER VECTOR --- Require the along-axis remainder to be orthogonal to derive the target squared-length denominator. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-505a502c372a}{Projection rejection and scalar components (02-Foundations-II, appendix-505a502c372a)} \\
539 & VECTOR REJECTION FROM ANOTHER VECTOR --- Rejection removes the projected component and enables tangent-plane movement. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-505a502c372a}{Projection rejection and scalar components (02-Foundations-II, appendix-505a502c372a)} \\
540 & SCALAR PROJECTION --- Signed scalar projection is length along a target direction, not the coefficient multiplying the target vector. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-505a502c372a}{Projection rejection and scalar components (02-Foundations-II, appendix-505a502c372a)} \\
541 & GRAM MATRIX CONCEPT --- Pairwise basis dots form a Gram metric; coordinates measure physical lengths through this matrix. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-3b863809f82a}{Gram matrices and coordinate metrics (02-Foundations-II, appendix-3b863809f82a)} \\
542 & Cross product definition in \(\mathbb R^3\) --- Reconstructed enrichment: perpendicular oriented-area vector definition precedes the supplied determinant derivation. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-2391db8052b1}{Cross products and oriented area (02-Foundations-II, appendix-2391db8052b1)} \\
543 & CROSS PRODUCT DETERMINANT DERIVATION --- Signed coordinate-plane minors yield the cross formula; direct expansion verifies perpendicularity. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-2391db8052b1}{Cross products and oriented area (02-Foundations-II, appendix-2391db8052b1)} \\
544 & CROSS PRODUCT GEOMETRIC MEANING --- Cross produces an area normal, not merely an arbitrary perpendicular direction. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-2391db8052b1}{Cross products and oriented area (02-Foundations-II, appendix-2391db8052b1)} \\
545 & CROSS-PRODUCT MAGNITUDE AND PARALLELOGRAM AREA --- Cross magnitude equals base times height; triangle area is half the parallelogram area. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-2391db8052b1}{Cross products and oriented area (02-Foundations-II, appendix-2391db8052b1)} \\
546 & CROSS-PRODUCT ORIENTATION AND THE RIGHT-HAND RULE --- Input order and a chosen positive orientation fix the cross sign; mirrored transforms add a determinant sign. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
547 & WHY THE CROSS PRODUCT IS SPECIAL TO THREE DIMENSIONS --- Ordinary graphics cross identifies bivectors with vectors in three dimensions; higher dimensions need qualifications. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
548 & SCALAR TRIPLE PRODUCT --- Dotting an oriented area with a third movement produces a signed scalar volume. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-5caa058bd9c7}{Triple products and signed volume (02-Foundations-II, appendix-5caa058bd9c7)} \\
549 & TRIPLE PRODUCT AS SIGNED VOLUME --- Triple product is parallelepiped volume; a four-point tetrahedron has one sixth of its absolute value. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-5caa058bd9c7}{Triple products and signed volume (02-Foundations-II, appendix-5caa058bd9c7)} \\
550 & VECTOR TRIPLE-PRODUCT IDENTITY --- Expand a nested cross into dot-weighted vectors; cross grouping is not associative. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-5caa058bd9c7}{Triple products and signed volume (02-Foundations-II, appendix-5caa058bd9c7)} \\
551 & ANGLES BETWEEN VECTORS --- Nonzero finite directions define an unsigned angle in the interval from zero to pi. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e569e60e18e6}{Robust vector angles (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e569e60e18e6)} \\
552 & ROBUST ANGLE COMPUTATION WITH \texttt{atan2} --- Scale first and use atan2 of cross length and dot; a signed angle additionally needs an oriented plane. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e569e60e18e6}{Robust vector angles (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e569e60e18e6)} \\
553 & REFLECTION OF A DIRECTION ACROSS A NORMAL --- Reverse the normal component to reflect across a plane; unit-normal and general-normal formulas differ. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-44e00863b4d5}{Reflection refraction and shading directions (02-Foundations-II, appendix-44e00863b4d5)} \\
554 & REFRACTION GEOMETRY FROM SNELL'S LAW --- Snell scales the tangent component; a negative transmitted squared cosine indicates total internal reflection. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-44e00863b4d5}{Reflection refraction and shading directions (02-Foundations-II, appendix-44e00863b4d5)} \\
555 & HALF-VECTOR CONSTRUCTION IN SHADING --- Normalize the sum of unit outward light/view directions; opposite inputs have no unique half-vector. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-44e00863b4d5}{Reflection refraction and shading directions (02-Foundations-II, appendix-44e00863b4d5)} \\
556 & TANGENT VECTORS ON A SURFACE --- Surface derivatives or triangle edges span tangent movement; texture tangents may retain physical stretch. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e31893dec985}{Surface tangents normals and bitangents (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e31893dec985)} \\
557 & NORMAL VECTORS ON A SURFACE --- Geometric normals measure perpendicularity; shading normals can differ and need their own contract. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e31893dec985}{Surface tangents normals and bitangents (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e31893dec985)} \\
558 & BITANGENT VECTORS --- Solve edge/UV equations for a second tangent; degenerate UVs and mirrored-island signs need explicit handling. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e31893dec985}{Surface tangents normals and bitangents (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e31893dec985)} \\
559 & ORTHONORMAL BASES --- Unit perpendicular axes give an identity Gram matrix and simple dot-based reconstruction. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-c39f1ed34271}{Orthonormal frames and Gram-Schmidt (02-Foundations-II, appendix-c39f1ed34271)} \\
560 & CONSTRUCTING AN ORTHONORMAL BASIS FROM ONE DIRECTION --- A least-aligned helper yields a nondegenerate frame from one direction, but does not ensure continuity. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-c39f1ed34271}{Orthonormal frames and Gram-Schmidt (02-Foundations-II, appendix-c39f1ed34271)} \\
561 & GRAM-SCHMIDT ORTHOGONALIZATION --- Repeated projection removal preserves span while producing perpendicular axes for independent generators. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-c39f1ed34271}{Orthonormal frames and Gram-Schmidt (02-Foundations-II, appendix-c39f1ed34271)} \\
562 & MODIFIED GRAM-SCHMIDT AND NUMERICAL STABILITY --- Measuring updated residuals changes rounding behavior; reorthogonalization cannot repair uncertain input rank. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-c39f1ed34271}{Orthonormal frames and Gram-Schmidt (02-Foundations-II, appendix-c39f1ed34271)} \\
563 & HANDEDNESS OF COORDINATE BASES --- Triple-product sign classifies an ordered basis relative to chosen orientation, not memory layout. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
564 & RIGHT-HANDED COORDINATE SYSTEMS --- Positive frame orientation is preserved by proper rotations and cyclic axis permutations. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
565 & LEFT-HANDED COORDINATE SYSTEMS --- A reflected frame has opposite orientation while preserving Euclidean distances; API conventions need separate statements. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
566 & ORIENTATION TESTS IN 2D USING SIGNED AREA --- Signed doubled triangle area supplies a two-dimensional orientation predicate with a documented axis convention. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
567 & ORIENTATION TESTS IN 3D USING TRIPLE PRODUCTS --- Four-point triple products decide plane side and coplanarity under an explicit ordering convention. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-87d7626c8d37}{Coordinate handedness and orientation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-87d7626c8d37)} \\
568 & BARYCENTRIC COORDINATES AS AFFINE WEIGHTS --- Triangle weights sum to one for affine meaning; nonnegative weights identify the convex interior. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-02b9d9a81dca}{Barycentric coordinates and interpolation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-02b9d9a81dca)} \\
569 & DERIVING BARYCENTRIC COORDINATES FOR A TRIANGLE --- Edge Gram solves or signed area ratios recover weights; degeneracy and off-plane residuals remain separate checks. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-02b9d9a81dca}{Barycentric coordinates and interpolation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-02b9d9a81dca)} \\
570 & INTERPOLATING ATTRIBUTES WITH BARYCENTRIC COORDINATES --- The same affine weights interpolate attributes; nonlinear encoding and unit length require additional policies. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-02b9d9a81dca}{Barycentric coordinates and interpolation (02-Foundations-II, appendix-02b9d9a81dca)} \\
571 & AFFINE COMBINATIONS VERSUS LINEAR COMBINATIONS --- Only sum-one weights preserve point translation; sum-zero weights describe a displacement. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0026ec42c9d5}{Convex combinations and convex hulls (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0026ec42c9d5)} \\
572 & CONVEX COMBINATIONS --- Nonnegative affine weights produce convex mixtures; extrapolation is affine but not convex. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0026ec42c9d5}{Convex combinations and convex hulls (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0026ec42c9d5)} \\
573 & CONVEX SETS --- A convex set contains every line segment between its members; a boundary need not be convex. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0026ec42c9d5}{Convex combinations and convex hulls (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0026ec42c9d5)} \\
574 & CONVEX HULL INTUITION --- A convex hull is all finite convex mixtures and may fill source concavities or holes. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0026ec42c9d5}{Convex combinations and convex hulls (02-Foundations-II, appendix-0026ec42c9d5)} \\
575 & MINKOWSKI SUM INTUITION --- Pairwise set-coordinate addition represents sweeps and expansions; symmetric robot dilation is a special case. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-215ac7099a82}{Minkowski sums and support mappings (02-Foundations-II, appendix-215ac7099a82)} \\
576 & MINKOWSKI DIFFERENCE INTUITION --- Collision difference means A plus reflected B here, not set subtraction; overlap is origin membership. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-215ac7099a82}{Minkowski sums and support mappings (02-Foundations-II, appendix-215ac7099a82)} \\
577 & SUPPORT FUNCTIONS OF CONVEX SHAPES --- Support scalar and support point differ; opposite-direction support queries construct difference extrema on demand. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-215ac7099a82}{Minkowski sums and support mappings (02-Foundations-II, appendix-215ac7099a82)} \\
578 & HOMOGENEOUS COORDINATES AS PROJECTIVE REPRESENTATIVES --- Nonzero common scale defines projective equivalence; finite representatives divide by their final coordinate. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-cb3b26862282}{Homogeneous coordinates and perspective division (02-Foundations-II, appendix-cb3b26862282)} \\
579 & POINTS AND DIRECTIONS THROUGH HOMOGENEOUS \(w\) --- Homogeneous one distinguishes points from zero-tagged directions under affine translation; zero tuple is invalid projectively. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-cb3b26862282}{Homogeneous coordinates and perspective division (02-Foundations-II, appendix-cb3b26862282)} \\
580 & NORMALIZED DEVICE-COORDINATE INTUITION AS A VECTOR-SPACE MAPPING --- NDC standardizes device-coordinate ranges, not length; perspective division is nonlinear and follows clipping. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-cb3b26862282}{Homogeneous coordinates and perspective division (02-Foundations-II, appendix-cb3b26862282)} \\
581 & DUAL VECTORS AND COVECTORS --- Covectors are linear scalar measurement rules; a metric hides their distinction from ordinary vectors. \hyperref[appendix-46da295c6787]{Covectors linear functionals and normal transforms} \\
582 & WHY SURFACE NORMALS TRANSFORM DIFFERENTLY FROM POSITIONS --- Preserve the normal's pairing with transformed tangents to derive inverse transpose; singular cases need policy. \hyperref[appendix-46da295c6787]{Covectors linear functionals and normal transforms} \\
583 & LINEAR FUNCTIONALS --- A linear functional distributes across weighted vector sums; its kernel contains movements it measures as zero. \hyperref[appendix-46da295c6787]{Covectors linear functionals and normal transforms} \\
584 & EXTERIOR-PRODUCT INTUITION --- Exterior algebra retains areas and volumes without requiring a vector-valued cross in every dimension. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e1960925d1b8}{Exterior products and geometric algebra (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e1960925d1b8)} \\
585 & WEDGE PRODUCT AS ORIENTED AREA CONCEPT --- Antisymmetric bilinear wedges encode oriented area; dependent generators have zero wedge. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e1960925d1b8}{Exterior products and geometric algebra (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e1960925d1b8)} \\
586 & GEOMETRIC-ALGEBRA MOTIVATION FOR GRAPHICS --- The geometric product combines dot and wedge; plane elements connect to complex-like rotation structure. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-e1960925d1b8}{Exterior products and geometric algebra (02-Foundations-II, appendix-e1960925d1b8)} \\
587 & FLOATING-POINT VECTOR STORAGE IN C++ --- Floating storage has bounded precision and target layout; trivial copying does not certify external representation. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b}{Vector storage SIMD and boundary layouts (02-Foundations-II, appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b)} \\
588 & VEC2 DATA STRUCTURE DESIGN --- A two-component value type needs initialization, safe access and explicit semantic roles. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-37a39984ba66}{Vector value types and component access (02-Foundations-II, appendix-37a39984ba66)} \\
589 & VEC3 DATA STRUCTURE DESIGN --- Three-component storage supports spatial operations but is not automatically a point, normal or unit direction. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-37a39984ba66}{Vector value types and component access (02-Foundations-II, appendix-37a39984ba66)} \\
590 & VEC4 DATA STRUCTURE DESIGN --- Four-component storage can serve distinct roles; homogeneous coordinates and quaternions require separate contracts. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-37a39984ba66}{Vector value types and component access (02-Foundations-II, appendix-37a39984ba66)} \\
591 & OPERATOR OVERLOADS FOR VECTOR ARITHMETIC --- Overloads should express unambiguous vector arithmetic; name dot, cross and componentwise multiplication. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-37a39984ba66}{Vector value types and component access (02-Foundations-II, appendix-37a39984ba66)} \\
592 & SWIZZLES AND COMPONENT ACCESS --- Read swizzles reorder components; writable swizzles need temporary-copy and duplicate-destination policies. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-37a39984ba66}{Vector value types and component access (02-Foundations-II, appendix-37a39984ba66)} \\
593 & ALIGNMENT OF VECTOR TYPES --- Measure alignment, size and stride; greater alignment may add padding and is not a performance guarantee. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b}{Vector storage SIMD and boundary layouts (02-Foundations-II, appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b)} \\
594 & PACKED VERSUS ALIGNED VEC3 REPRESENTATIONS --- Compact storage and aligned compute types can coexist with explicit conversion at boundaries. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b}{Vector storage SIMD and boundary layouts (02-Foundations-II, appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b)} \\
595 & SIMD IMPLEMENTATION OF VECTOR ARITHMETIC --- SIMD within one vector differs from SIMD across objects; layouts, reductions and FMA affect throughput and results. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b}{Vector storage SIMD and boundary layouts (02-Foundations-II, appendix-26bf6bf2fb8b)} \\
596 & APPROXIMATE VECTOR EQUALITY --- Exact, componentwise, distance, angular and ULP comparisons answer different questions; closeness is not transitive. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b5b83329215b}{Vector tolerances and finite-state diagnostics (02-Foundations-II, appendix-b5b83329215b)} \\
597 & DEBUGGING NaN VECTORS --- Catch the first invalid input or operation using finite checks; NaN propagation can silently skip decisions. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b5b83329215b}{Vector tolerances and finite-state diagnostics (02-Foundations-II, appendix-b5b83329215b)} \\
598 & VECTOR INVARIANTS IN ENGINE APIs --- Validated wrappers establish finite/unit promises and operations must preserve or explicitly drop them. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-380a220e2529}{Coordinate-space types and validated invariants (02-Foundations-II, appendix-380a220e2529)} \\
599 & DISTINGUISHING WORLD-SPACE AND LOCAL-SPACE VECTOR TYPES --- Space-tagged points, vectors and transforms reject broad frame mistakes; dynamic local-frame identity remains a separate issue. \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-380a220e2529}{Coordinate-space types and validated invariants (02-Foundations-II, appendix-380a220e2529)} \\
600 & Reconstructing a Production-Ready \texttt{Vec3} Library from First Principles --- Reconstruct the scalar library from the mathematics, then verify contracts with edge cases and seeded properties. \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#A\%20complete\%20scalar\%20laboratory\%20makes\%20the\%20story\%20executable/}{Inline capstone and tests} \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{XR01 - Independent seeing and representation source}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-xr01---independent-seeing-and-representation-source}

This source is outside the 3,000-part curriculum. Module labels below preserve the attachment's coverage without assigning it main part numbers. XR01 is one accepted source in a separate anticipated 3--4-source continuous series; future inputs are pending. Main coverage is recorded separately in the current Phase 7 ledger above, with the Part 542 reconstruction and missing 635--650 sources identified.

XR01/1 - Light & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-215c2570199b}{Light spectra and radiometry (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-215c2570199b)} and \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-96da0b153b77}{Reflectance and rendering equation (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-96da0b153b77)}: spectra/receptor response, units, BRDF and transport assumptions. \\
XR01/2 - Eye & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-7281c15ac44a}{Retinal encoding and receptive fields (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-7281c15ac44a)}: anatomy, receptors, phototransduction, center-surround and cortical processing. \\
XR01/3 - Acuity & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-7d356c396d1a}{Visual angle and calibrated rays (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-7d356c396d1a)} and \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b491fc48c54b}{Visual acuity and eccentricity (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-b491fc48c54b)}: angular units, local/average PPD, eccentricity, MAR conventions. \\
XR01/4 - Optics & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f90cee1be7a4}{Optical focus and binocular conflict (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-f90cee1be7a4)} and \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-8b76b0efd27f}{PSF OTF and MTF (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-8b76b0efd27f)}: refraction, focus, diffraction, distortion and contrast transfer. \\
XR01/5 - Perception & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b3aa9e8e01ad}{Contrast sensitivity adaptation and masking (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-b3aa9e8e01ad)}, \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-a6f2c4fe1996}{Eye movements and display-time gaze (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-a6f2c4fe1996)}, \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-1759f03e8ce7}{Psychophysics and threshold inference (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-1759f03e8ce7)}: task, timing and detection models. \\
XR01/6 - Signals & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-1077ea8fc737}{Continuous signals and discrete samples (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-1077ea8fc737)}: domains, timestamps, sampling versus quantization and error assumptions. \\
XR01/7 - Fourier & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b60686ba06a1}{Fourier analysis and spectral phase (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-b60686ba06a1)}: basis projection, DFT/FFT, phase, bins, leakage and amplitude conventions. \\
XR01/8 - Sampling & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-6a7869dd79a6}{Sampling aliasing and reconstruction (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-6a7869dd79a6)} and \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-70895739febb}{Spatiotemporal sampling and retinal motion (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-70895739febb)}: spectral replicas, sinc, prefiltering, moving-pattern frequency. \\
XR01/9 - Filtering & \href{../03-Visual-Computing-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-d5b3c692d9f0}{Convolution and filter footprints (03-Visual-Computing-I, appendix-d5b3c692d9f0)} and \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b677a85ba76f}{Multiscale texture filtering (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-b677a85ba76f)}: kernels, separability, reconstruction, ringing, footprints and pyramids. \\
XR01/10 - Antialiasing & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-4192b2bdd21e}{Antialiasing and sample allocation (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-4192b2bdd21e)}: pixel integration, coverage versus shading and SSAA/MSAA distinctions. \\
XR01/11 - Temporal & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0727340a4428}{Temporal reprojection and history validation (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-0727340a4428)}: weights, motion/jitter conventions, disocclusion and changing-quality history. \\
XR01/12 - Color & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-a9f83ad40792}{Color encoding and linear-light operations (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-a9f83ad40792)}: transfer/primaries, XYZ, relative luminance, HDR and distinct noise mechanisms. \\
XR01/13 - Quality & \href{../04-Visual-Computing-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-b2c1846508bf}{Perceptual metrics and display calibration (04-Visual-Computing-II, appendix-b2c1846508bf)}: reference, MSE/PSNR/SSIM, gaze weighting and model limits. \\
XR01/14 - Papers & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/University\%20-\%20Worlds,\%20Images\%20and\%20Inference\%20-\%20Graphics,\%20Vision\%20and\%20Photography\#Read\%20research\%20as\%20a\%20chain\%20of\%20questions,\%20methods\%20and\%20bounded\%20conclusions/}{Bounded paper-reading anchors}; coefficients/study details not fully inspected remain source-reported. \\

\section{M007 - Parts 101-112}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m007---parts-101-112}

The supplied source has exactly twelve parts, 101--112. The revised M007 was integrated into the same coverage, not counted as new parts. M008 supplies 113--126, M009 supplies 127--147, and M010 supplies 148--160. Volume 02 is 4/12 accepted sources, not completed Parts 101--300.

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
101 & \hyperref[appendix-806686359e1b]{Translation units and linking} --- Source → preprocessing → semantic compilation → object/BMI → link. A declaration can permit compilation while an absent implementation fails linking; BMI is not object code. \\
102 & \hyperref[appendix-2e4d494c2d94]{Declarations and definitions} --- Every definition is a declaration, not conversely. extern int x declares; int x=0 defines. Forward class is incomplete; fixed-underlying opaque enum is complete. \\
103 & \hyperref[appendix-49d27bc9d9a5]{One Definition Rule} --- Multiple translation units must describe coherent entities. Header guards are per-TU; ordinary external definitions and inline/template exceptions have different constraints. \\
104 & \hyperref[appendix-b12b31759da1]{Scopes and name lookup} --- Scope belongs to a name; qualified lookup selects a context; hiding changes lookup without deleting the hidden object. ADL adds associated candidates. \\
105 & \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0b104bcba06b}{Storage duration and object lifetime (05-Engine-I, appendix-0b104bcba06b)} --- Automatic/static/thread/dynamic classify storage. A block-scope static outlives the block; a local pointer and its dynamic pointee have distinct durations. \\
106 & \hyperref[appendix-a82f9c438857]{Object construction and storage reuse} --- Sufficient aligned storage and completed initialization establish an ordinary object lifetime. destroy\_at can end the object while the byte buffer remains; casts do not construct arbitrary objects. \\
107 & \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f1581457d322}{Object identity and generation handles (05-Engine-I, appendix-f1581457d322)} --- Equal values need not be one object, and reused addresses host different occupants. A slot/generation needs live-registry validation, not merely a non-sentinel index. \\
108 & \hyperref[appendix-cc04044b5782]{Fixed-width integer types} --- sizeof(char)=1 C++ byte; CHAR\_BIT≥8. int has minimum capacity, not a universal32-bit width. Exact/least/fast and size\_t express different requirements. \\
109 & \hyperref[appendix-23640fcee147]{Scoped enums and validated domains} --- Scoped enumerators and no implicit integer conversion reduce accidents. Explicit underlying width does not validate received codes; states and combinable flags need separate designs. \\
110 & \hyperref[appendix-b7174c179542]{Aggregate initialization} --- C++20/23 aggregate criteria are not the older user-provided-constructor rule. Designators follow member declaration order. Braces may select initializer-list or ordinary constructors instead. \\
111 & \hyperref[appendix-8e5214819615]{Class access and invariants} --- struct defaults members and bases public; class defaults them private. Both are class types. Record/invariant intent is convention, not a syntactic capability limit. \\
112 & \hyperref[appendix-958870a64c53]{Standard-layout and trivial copyability} --- Alignment creates internal/tail padding. Traits do not imply array traversal, packing, serialization or GPU compatibility. Named x/y/z require explicit indexing or actual array storage. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{M008 - Parts 113-126}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m008---parts-113-126}

All fourteen supplied parts are accepted. Source numbering audits coverage; it does not divide the teaching story. M009 now develops parameter passing and value transfer in Parts127--147.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and concrete reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
113 & \hyperref[appendix-d6d562ca9fbb]{References and aliases} --- Binding establishes an alias; assignment acts on the referent. A reference-to-pointer can reseat that pointer, unlike a reference to its pointee. \\
114 & \hyperref[appendix-37856d997bb0]{Reference binding and converted temporaries} --- Type/category select the referent. const int\& can track the original; const double\& can bind a converted snapshot. Arrays keep extent through array references; bit-fields require special handling. \\
115 & \hyperref[appendix-a978b29bbbb0]{Temporary lifetime extension} and \hyperref[appendix-0104b704fb11]{Move casts and forwarding references} --- Direct local binding can extend a temporary, not transitive function-return aliases. Braces/parentheses/new exceptions matter; move is a cast, and deduced T\&\& forwarding collapses references. \\
116 & \hyperref[appendix-1121b30eaeb0]{Value categories and materialization} --- lvalue/xvalue identify, prvalue produces; glvalue and rvalue overlap at xvalue. A named T\&\& is an lvalue in ordinary use. decltype(name) differs from decltype((name)). \\
117 & \hyperref[appendix-6af5921bc853]{Pointer arithmetic and provenance} --- A pointer is an access path, not ownership. Null/object/one-past/invalid states differ; dereference requires live, aligned, type-accessible in-range storage. \\
118 & \hyperref[appendix-afae48c31800]{Null pointers and optional access} --- nullptr distinguishes pointer absence from integer zero. Typed null deletion is valid; bare delete nullptr is ill-formed. Non-null does not prove lifetime. \\
119 & \hyperref[appendix-3f7e15effba6]{Named members versus array elements} --- Array-relative positions zero through N may be formed; only zero through N-1 dereferenced. Same-array subtraction must fit ptrdiff\_t. Adjacent members do not authorize array indexing. \\
120 & \hyperref[appendix-6af5921bc853]{Pointer arithmetic and provenance} --- A pointer's storage relationship is not manufactured by a plausible integer. Specified round trips do not resurrect occupants or authorize arbitrary arithmetic; P3407 is discussion, not C++23 law. \\
121 & \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-f9b8b9188c7f}{Borrow invalidation and logical identity (05-Engine-I, appendix-f9b8b9188c7f)} --- Vector reallocation can dangle a borrow; swap assignment can leave a live slot with a different logical owner. Valid handles do not certify previous component references. \\
122 & \hyperref[appendix-aea529eb5175]{Deferred captures and borrowed views} --- Reference members, spans, string views and captured this pointers require referent lifetime. Copying a view does not own its buffer; an owning capture supplies a different contract. \\
123 & \hyperref[appendix-c25d60fed375]{Const qualification and shallow constness} and \hyperref[appendix-5d4252a42886]{Auto decltype and reference collapsing} --- Read-only access is not global immutability. Top-level const is often dropped by value deduction; actual const objects cannot be made writable by casts. \\
124 & \hyperref[appendix-5e1a7561cd98]{Multilevel pointer qualification} --- Separate pointer and pointee qualification. int** to const int** would enable unsafe injection; intermediate const blocks it. Const span descriptors do not imply const elements. \\
125 & \hyperref[appendix-d42039b1d6f7]{Ref-qualified accessors} --- const member functions restrict the object path; \&/\&\& constrain caller category. Persistent getters can borrow; both mutable and const temporary getters need value/deleted policies. \\
126 & \hyperref[appendix-f5593256bcaf]{Logical constness and mutable caches} --- Eligible mutable members permit representation changes under const access. Cache/source invariants require invalidation and synchronization; return protected snapshots. Mutable closures are not automatically thread-safe. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{M009 - Parts 127-147}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m009---parts-127-147}

The supplied source contains exactly twenty-one parts. At acceptance, it extended Volume02 to Parts101--147; M010 subsequently added Parts148--160. Teaching headings remain concept-led; exact coverage is recorded in the current ledger.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
127 & \hyperref[appendix-d5908f75721f]{Parameter passing and sink contracts} --- Argument expressions initialize separate value-parameter objects. Lvalue/xvalue/prvalue routes differ; a sink retains/transforms a value, and copied pointer members still alias. \\
128 & \hyperref[appendix-d6d562ca9fbb]{References and aliases} --- Mutable/const/rvalue reference parameters borrow with different access and consumption contracts. Aliased source/destination references can change a sequential update's result. \\
129 & \hyperref[appendix-d5908f75721f]{Parameter passing and sink contracts} --- Pointer parameters copy pointer values; T*\& or pointer-output protocols can reseat caller state. Null optionality must be documented; array parameters adjust to pointers without extent enforcement. \\
130 & \hyperref[appendix-b08d3788c5c6]{Return values and copy elision} --- Result objects can be constructed directly; NRVO is optional while appropriate same-type prvalue initialization requires no intermediate transfer. Eligible local/parameter return expressions implicitly move in C++23. \\
131 & \hyperref[appendix-d42039b1d6f7]{Ref-qualified accessors} and \hyperref[appendix-a978b29bbbb0]{Temporary lifetime extension} --- Reference/view results retain owner lifetime/invalidation obligations. Temporary getters require policies for const rvalues too; decltype(auto) can return a reference through parentheses. \\
132 & \hyperref[appendix-7f8ec83d0886]{Constructors and invariant establishment} --- Members/bases initialize before the body. Initializers establish state; body assignments replace it. Constructor failure and factory error results are different protocols. \\
133 & \hyperref[appendix-ba8c1a9fe03f]{Default and value initialization} --- Default, zero, aggregate and value initialization differ. First-declaration defaulting is not an empty user-provided body; braces do not universally initialize every scalar. \\
134 & \hyperref[appendix-3cb49709605a]{Member order and delegating constructors} --- Declaration order governs members regardless of colon-list spelling. Provider-before-dependent order establishes correct construction/reverse cleanup. \\
135 & \hyperref[appendix-3cb49709605a]{Member order and delegating constructors} --- Delegation centralizes a target initialization path, then runs its own body. The target is the sole initializer; cycles are ill-formed. \\
136 & \hyperref[appendix-9bfe377cef7a]{Destruction and partial construction} --- Destructor body precedes reverse subobject cleanup; failed ordinary construction destroys completed subobjects. Delegating-target completion changes the whole-object cleanup case. \\
137 & \hyperref[appendix-1340cd1ce582]{Copy semantics and resource identity} --- New-object copying follows members' semantic operations; equal-sign initialization is not assignment. Pointer/reference copies preserve associations rather than clone referents. \\
138 & \hyperref[appendix-bea6beb5350f]{Assignment and exception guarantees} --- Existing-object replacement must handle old ownership, self-aliasing and failure. Prepare before commit; reference/const members can delete generated assignment. \\
139 & \hyperref[appendix-1340cd1ce582]{Copy semantics and resource identity} --- Deep/shallow are contract choices, not good/bad labels. Pixel values may duplicate, handles identify, graph clones remap; copy-on-write adds mutation/lifetime/synchronization costs. \\
140 & \hyperref[appendix-5636651017c2]{Move operations and moved-from states} --- New-object moves can take resources and repair source state; scalar/inline members need not become cheaper. Ordinary constructors alone do not suppress implicit move generation. \\
141 & \hyperref[appendix-5636651017c2]{Move operations and moved-from states} --- Move assignment also handles the destination's former resource and self-move. Allocator policy can prevent constant-time adoption. \\
142 & \hyperref[appendix-0104b704fb11]{Move casts and forwarding references} --- move changes category, not ownership itself; const remains const. Explicit local-return moves can inhibit NRVO; move\_if\_noexcept selects a transfer access path. \\
143 & \hyperref[appendix-5636651017c2]{Move operations and moved-from states} --- Source lifetime continues after moving. Valid-but-unspecified is not destroyed or undefined; operation preconditions still apply. A custom empty source needs an explicit maintained invariant. \\
144 & \hyperref[appendix-b08d3788c5c6]{Return values and copy elision} --- Immovable types can use guaranteed direct result construction; a named-local fallback still needs usable operations. Accessible destruction remains required. \\
145 & \hyperref[appendix-7679107d057e]{Rules of Three Five and Zero} --- Destructor/copy constructor/copy assignment are coordinated raw-owner decisions; copying may instead be deleted. It is a design heuristic, not a compiler mandate. \\
146 & \hyperref[appendix-42e02a12c694]{Special member generation and traits} --- Five adds both moves. User-declared versus user-provided matters; a defaulted destructor suppresses dedicated implicit moves, while move-constructible traits can still classify copying as viable. \\
147 & \hyperref[appendix-7679107d057e]{Rules of Three Five and Zero} --- Compose tested member owners rather than repeat transfer/release logic. Zero does not forbid ordinary constructors or prove cross-member invariants; \hyperref[appendix-adfd6397af8d]{Relocation versus move} remains a versioned research bridge. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{M010 - Parts 148-160}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m010---parts-148-160}

M010 continues the same Volume 02 story from Rule-of-Zero composition into the concrete ownership and lifetime protocols underneath it. The source specifies Parts 148--160. Repository paths named in the pasted lesson are source-provided examples and remain \textbf{To verify} until their repositories/revisions are inspected. These entries identify coverage; the expanded teaching narrative is in \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20a\%20live\%20object\%20to\%20responsible\%20resource\%20ownership}{Volume 02}.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and concrete reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
148 & \hyperref[appendix-69739d3ab49b]{RAII and resource handles} --- Bind matched acquisition/release responsibility to a live owner; scope exit handles early return and unwinding. External asynchronous use can require deferred reclamation. \\
149 & \hyperref[appendix-69739d3ab49b]{RAII and resource handles} --- Each fully acquired resource must immediately enter an owner; completed subobjects clean up if construction fails. CPU ownership does not alone establish GPU completion. \\
150 & \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-d52e4486f527}{Ownership and non-owning views (05-Engine-I, appendix-d52e4486f527)} --- Ownership means responsibility for ending use/lifetime; a borrow provides access without extending lifetime. \\
151 & \hyperref[appendix-51602cabe815]{Exclusive and shared smart-pointer ownership} --- \texttt{unique\_ptr} is a move-only exclusive owner; \texttt{get()} borrows while \texttt{release()} transfers responsibility. \\
152 & \hyperref[appendix-51602cabe815]{Exclusive and shared smart-pointer ownership} --- A custom deleter pairs a native handle with its actual release protocol; deleter state/type can affect representation and conversion safety. \\
153 & \hyperref[appendix-51602cabe815]{Exclusive and shared smart-pointer ownership} --- \texttt{shared\_ptr} copies join a control block's strong ownership group; object and control-block lifetimes may differ. \\
154 & \hyperref[appendix-51602cabe815]{Exclusive and shared smart-pointer ownership} --- Reference-count operations have costs; control-block coordination does not synchronize arbitrary pointee operations. \\
155 & \hyperref[appendix-b9548675eb2b]{Weak observation and ownership cycles} --- A weak edge avoids extending object lifetime; \texttt{lock()} safely attempts to obtain a temporary strong owner and can break cycles. \\
156 & \hyperref[appendix-b9548675eb2b]{Weak observation and ownership cycles} --- \texttt{enable\_shared\_from\_this} joins an existing owner group; never build an independent \texttt{shared\_ptr} control block from \texttt{this}. \\
157 & \hyperref[appendix-f56c3e43fd4e]{Placement construction and explicit lifetime management} --- Allocation functions acquire raw storage; new-expressions combine allocation and initialization. Size, alignment, construction, failure cleanup and deallocation must match. \\
158 & \hyperref[appendix-f56c3e43fd4e]{Placement construction and explicit lifetime management} --- Arrays require correct count-aware construction/destruction and matching \texttt{new{[}{]}}/\texttt{delete{[}{]}}; containers usually own this protocol more safely. \\
159 & \hyperref[appendix-f56c3e43fd4e]{Placement construction and explicit lifetime management} --- Placement construction/\texttt{construct\_at} begins an object lifetime in adequate pre-existing storage; it does not allocate that storage. \\
160 & \hyperref[appendix-f56c3e43fd4e]{Placement construction and explicit lifetime management} --- Explicit destruction ends an occupant's lifetime, not necessarily storage. Reuse, transparent replacement and laundering have distinct conditions. \\
\end{longtable}
}

The source discusses resource owners, smart pointers, manual allocation, placement construction, array ownership and lifetime restart. Its repository examples (\texttt{KairoRenderer/VulkanBuffer.cppm}, \texttt{Maveb/.../MetalPtr.hpp}) are \textbf{not} represented as live-inspected code findings here.

\section{M011 --- Parts 161--180: a request changes shape and a backend changes implementation}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m011-parts-161180-a-request-changes-shape-and-a-backend-changes-implementation}

\textbf{Source status:} authored reconstruction from the canonical integrated 180-chunk / original 3,000-part curriculum contract, \textbf{not} a received M011 teaching transcript. \textbf{Teaching placement:} \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20one\%20owned\%20resource\%20to\%20many\%20requests\%20and\%20a\%20replaceable\%20backend}{the continuous C++ narrative}, immediately after the M010 ownership chapter and before the API-publication chapter. The concrete example follows a typed renderer request through a polymorphic backend; it does not substitute a dictionary entry for each numbered part.

161 & A command record can hold overlapping alternatives but not several simultaneously active objects. & Raw union active-member and lifetime rules; trivial union assignment in tested C++20 lab. \\
162 & A request must carry its active alternative rather than guess an interpretation from bytes. & \texttt{std::variant}, \texttt{get\_if}, \texttt{holds\_alternative} and \texttt{visit}, replacement and exceptional empty state; executable request-state checks. \\
163 & An editor should depend on the backend's promised operations, not the native implementation. & Base/derived interface contract contrasted with composition and closed alternatives. \\
164 & A derived object contains a base subobject with its own identity and layout constraints. & Static versus dynamic object view; no invented vptr byte layout. \\
165 & A base reference must call the selected backend's implementation. & Virtual dispatch with observable CountingBackend result in the lab. \\
166 & Indirection has implementation and performance consequences, but no mandated vtable layout. & Implementation-model explanation, devirtualization caveat and proposed measurement controls. \\
167 & A backend contract needs operations without constructing the incomplete base. & Pure virtual operation and abstract interface; concrete derived override. \\
168 & An owner typed as base must destroy the concrete derived object correctly. & Virtual destructor through \texttt{std::unique\_ptr\textless{}Backend\textgreater{}}; derived-destruction assertion. \\
169 & Copying a derived object into a base value can erase its specialized identity. & \texttt{MeshObject} / \texttt{NamedObject} slicing contrasted with a base reference; tested. \\
170 & A backend can implement multiple independent interfaces, creating distinct base views. & Multiple inheritance, ambiguity and subobject-adjustment explanation. \\
171 & A diamond may duplicate a common base unless virtual inheritance shares it. & \texttt{Diamond} with two paths through a virtual \texttt{Root}; identical subobject address test. \\
172 & Upcasting to a public base is different from a downcast that assumes an actual derived object. & \texttt{static\_cast} guarantees and the conditions it does not dynamically test. \\
173 & A diagnostic needs to ask whether the runtime backend really has a concrete type. & \texttt{dynamic\_cast} success and failed pointer cast tested; RTTI and design cautions. \\
174 & Byte reinterpretation must not masquerade as object construction or safe aliasing. & \texttt{reinterpret\_cast} limitations; byte views and \texttt{bit\_cast} alternatives. \\
175 & Removing const qualification is not the same as making a const object writable. & \texttt{const\_cast} and genuine-const/ownership rules. \\
176 & Operators should communicate a domain operation and preserve invariants. & Resource handles versus durations and inappropriate hidden side effects. \\
177 & A handle's equality includes its generation, not merely a reused slot. & Defaulted \texttt{operator==} derived from \texttt{\textless{}=\textgreater{}} and two identity assertions. \\
178 & Identifiers need meaningful ordering; NaN can make floating comparison unordered. & Strong integer ordering, partial floating ordering and strict-weak-order concerns. \\
179 & Overload selection is a compile-time decision based on arguments and conversions. & Integer/double overload calls tested; ambiguity and conversion cautions. \\
180 & Virtual implementation selection and default-argument binding happen at different times. & Base default 2 with derived body produces 20; derived default 7 produces 70; tested. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Executed reference, scope and limits.} \texttt{tools/labs/backend\_choice\_lab.cpp} was compiled with GCC 14.2.0 on Linux as C++20 using \texttt{-Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer} and executed with all assertions passing. The committed source was independently compared byte-for-byte to that locally executed source by FNV-1a checksum (both \texttt{0x22332ec3}, length 6,431 bytes). This checksum comparison establishes identical bytes for the compared artifact; the compiler run remains a single compiler/platform witness, not every possible C++ implementation. The example intentionally simulates renderer behavior and contains no native graphics API execution. Current Kairo and AI-provider repositories were \textbf{not} re-inspected here; the listed original code-lab paths are curriculum \emph{targets}, not verified implementation claims.

\textbf{Completion classification:} 20 canonical part titles mapped to a developed narrative and one tested representative laboratory; not marked as 20 individually demonstrated learner mastery gates or a fully verified Kairo code audit. Additional compiler/platform checks, user reconstruction, project-specific anchors, and production performance comparisons remain explicit.

\section{M012 --- Parts 181--200: typed asset requests, deferred callbacks and transactional publication}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m012-parts-181200-typed-asset-requests-deferred-callbacks-and-transactional-publication}

\textbf{Provenance and status:} authored reconstruction using the original 3,000-part contract's exact M012 titles (not a received M012 lesson transcript). The material belongs directly in \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20an\%20accepted\%20render\%20request\%20to\%20a\%20resource\%20that\%20survives\%20failures\%20and\%20callbacks}{the continuing computing-foundations narrative}. It follows the existing M011 backend selection chapter and precedes the pre-existing general API-contract chapter. The 20 original identifiers remain a coverage map, \textbf{not} a substitute for the connected explanation.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Original part / exact source title
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Why the renderer story requires it and where the evidence sits
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
181 · Implicit conversions and converting constructors & A raw integer is not automatically a meaningful byte count; the chapter contrasts implicit construction with deliberate unit conversion. \\
182 · explicit constructors and conversion control & \texttt{explicit\ ByteCount(std::size\_t)} prevents mistaken copy-initialization; compile-time checks verify the conversion restriction, not range validity. \\
183 · User-defined conversion operators & \texttt{explicit\ operator\ std::size\_t()} requires deliberate extraction; contextual conversions and overload effects are explained. \\
184 · Function pointers & A synchronous C-style progress callback receives a function pointer plus borrowed context; the lab checks the invocation. \\
185 · Member-function pointers & A \texttt{Reporter} member pointer requires a particular live instance; the lab calls the member through an object. \\
186 · std::function type erasure and overhead & The copyable callable wrapper hides target type, not allocation/copy/lifetime cost; the lab checks an owning target and empty invocation. \\
187 · Callable objects and functors & \texttt{AddBytes::operator()} carries state for a progress update; pointer-member lifetime remains a borrow. \\
188 · Lambdas as generated closure types & The closure object combines an operation with stored or referenced captured state; no concrete closure byte layout is claimed. \\
189 · Lambda capture by value & Snapshot and shared-owner captures show the difference between copied scalar state and an owned lifetime. \\
190 · Lambda capture by reference and lifetime hazards & A reference capture reads later mutations only while its referent lives; invalid async scene callback is explained but not executed. \\
191 · Exceptions: throw, propagation, catch, unwinding & The deliberately injected failure after resource creation unwinds a private local owner; typed result conversion occurs at the public boundary. \\
192 · noexcept and optimization/contracts & \texttt{Resource} destructor and move operations express non-escaping exception promises; compile-time traits connect to vector insertion guarantees. \\
193 · Strong, basic, and no-throw exception guarantees & Prepare privately, publish once, compare stored state and native-handle counts after failure; guarantee is limited to stated observable state. \\
194 · Error codes versus exceptions in engine code & Empty input and operational failure map to typed \texttt{UploadError}; exception categories remain an implementation detail within the bounded example. \\
195 · Assertions as programmer-error checks & The lab's \texttt{verify} is active under \texttt{NDEBUG}; external input checks and internal invariant failures use distinct policies. \\
196 · Undefined behavior and optimizer assumptions & Invalid lifetime, out-of-bounds, aliasing and data-race examples are explained, never deliberately run as undefined behavior. \\
197 · Strict aliasing rules & A raw file cannot be safely interpreted as a typed struct merely by pointer cast; object type-access and lifetime conditions are explicit. \\
198 · Object representation, bytes, and std::bit\_cast & IEEE binary32 \texttt{bit\_cast} is tested under platform assertions; a separate little-endian serializer establishes portable file bytes. \\
199 · Trivial, trivially copyable, and standard-layout properties & \texttt{WireHeader} traits are checked without falsely asserting its raw storage is a six-byte file layout; differing type categories are explained. \\
200 · Designing a resource-owning C++ type from blank memory to safe API & \texttt{Resource} develops empty/owning/moved-from states, matching release, deleted copy, noexcept move and a device-outlives-owner contract; AssetStore adds a failure-atomic publication boundary. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Executable verification:} \href{tools/labs/safe_asset_pipeline_lab.cpp}{C++20 reference laboratory}, 275 lines and 11,473 bytes. Exact committed Git blob \texttt{bc811fb2c659b635af290d2f3abb58a6c38a06fc} matches the locally executed lab's Git hash. Local SHA-256: \texttt{32e93bc026677e074aaa77711c7b3fea721e0f582e64eb5d86c2d740425df92c}. Ran successfully with GCC 14.2.0, \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}; also compiled and ran under Clang 17.0.0 with warning flags and GCC release-style \texttt{-DNDEBUG\ -O2}. Outputs reported \texttt{safe\_asset\_pipeline\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}. The tests witness resource counts, strong-state preservation under one injected failure, callback lifetimes, overload shapes, \texttt{bit\_cast}, and explicit endian encoding; they do not prove all exception interleavings, driver synchronization, exact allocation costs, thread safety or raw Kairo implementation behavior.

\textbf{Research/code audit still pending:} the curriculum suggests \texttt{KairoAssets/AtomicFile.cppm} and \texttt{KairoRenderer/VulkanDevice.cppm}. Neither the live source revisions nor user-project build/test results have been inspected here. Source-verified language discussions: \href{https://eel.is/c++draft/except.spec}{C++ draft exception specifications}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/functional/function}{\texttt{std::function} callable requirements}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/numeric/bit_cast}{\texttt{std::bit\_cast}}, and \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/object}{C++ object representation}. Their existence does not claim exhaustive normative clause analysis.

\textbf{Gate distinction:} all 20 original titles have integrated teaching and one representative tested pipeline. This is \emph{authored coverage}, not a claim of 20 separate tested programs, universally exhaustive failure injection, independent user reconstruction or a received transcript. The next unreceived canonical chunk is M013, Parts 201--216; earlier original-source gaps elsewhere remain pending.

\section{M013 --- Parts 201--216: generic asset preparation with constrained APIs}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m013-parts-201216-generic-asset-preparation-with-constrained-apis}

\textbf{Provenance:} authored reconstruction of the exact original M013 part titles from the integrated 180-chunk / 3,000-part contract, \textbf{not} a received M013 source transcript. The actual lesson is the continuous \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#One\%20asset\%20pipeline,\%20many\%20asset\%20types\%20—\%20generic\%20code\%20without\%20accidental\%20promises}{asset-pipeline narrative}, placed directly after M012 rather than scattered into 16 separate disconnected definitions.

Original part and authoritative title

201 · Function templates and type deduction & One \texttt{asset\_bytes} operation handles multiple supported asset types; \texttt{T} is deduced from actual mesh and texture calls. \\
202 · Class templates and instantiated types & \texttt{AssetDepot\textless{}T,\ Capacity\textgreater{}} represents independent statically typed storage for mesh and shader records. \\
203 · Non-type template parameters & \texttt{Capacity} and texture \texttt{Channels} are compile-time values affecting type identity, storage and byte-size policy. \\
204 · Template specialization & A complete \texttt{AssetTraits\textless{}Mesh\textgreater{}} specialization supplies mesh-specific byte-size behavior. \\
205 · Partial specialization & \texttt{AssetTraits\textless{}Texture\textless{}Channels\textgreater{}\textgreater{}} supplies one rule for the family of supported channel counts. \\
206 · Template argument deduction & \texttt{const\ T\&} and \texttt{Args\&\&} follow distinct deduction rules; callers need not manually name every deduced type. \\
207 · Reference collapsing rules & All four \texttt{\&}/\texttt{\&\&} combinations are derived, with lvalue and rvalue assertions in the lab. \\
208 · Forwarding references & The function-template \texttt{Args\&\&...} preserves incoming lvalues and rvalues across a generic API boundary. \\
209 · Perfect forwarding with std::forward & \texttt{std::forward\textless{}Args\textgreater{}(args)...} selects the intended copy or move constructor for \texttt{Mesh}; both paths are tested. \\
210 · Variadic templates and parameter packs & \texttt{total\_bytes(const\ T\&...\ assets)} accepts a heterogeneous, compile-time-checked group, including an empty pack. \\
211 · Fold expressions & A built-in comma fold performs checked left-to-right byte accumulation; 12+48+15=75 and zero-argument cases tested. \\
212 · SFINAE and substitution failure & \texttt{HasByteSize} uses \texttt{std::void\_t}, \texttt{decltype} and \texttt{declval} to detect a valid trait expression without a hard error in its substitution context. \\
213 · Concepts as compile-time semantic constraints & Named \texttt{Uploadable} accepts mesh and supported textures but not an unknown asset; tested with \texttt{static\_assert}. \\
214 · requires expressions & Compound requirements check the label's conversion and exact byte-size result type, but do not execute runtime validation. \\
215 · requires clauses & \texttt{AssetDepot::emplace} requires \texttt{std::constructible\_from\textless{}T,\ Args\&\&...\textgreater{}}; invalid integer-to-mesh construction rejected at compile time. \\
216 · Constrained overload resolution & \texttt{route} has a generic fallback and a \texttt{Uploadable}-constrained overload; correct static routing tested for supported and unknown types. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Actual compiled witness:} \href{tools/labs/generic_asset_pipeline_lab.cpp}{C++20 reference laboratory}, 249 lines / 10,302 bytes (ASCII), SHA-256 \texttt{a5db53514d74ae247b4ac42fafb8504ae701bd0746141ab4861ce874f496e30f}, Git blob \texttt{e673da4c89e50114b3e49c5b1b32dda7bf019ec8}, verified byte-identical with the executed source. It passed three actual builds: GCC 14.2.0 \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}; Clang 17.0.0 \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}; GCC 14.2.0 \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O2\ -DNDEBUG\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}. Each executable printed \texttt{generic\_asset\_pipeline\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}. An initial compiler warning from the empty pack under GCC \texttt{-Werror} was corrected by explicitly marking the sometimes-unused fold helper \texttt{{[}{[}maybe\_unused{]}{]}}; the final committed blob is the passing version.

\textbf{What was checked:} compile-time admission/rejection; lvalue copying versus rvalue transfer; capacity rejection without state change; failed shader construction without publication; unused slot reuse; traits for 3/4 channels; numerical overflow rejection; batch sum and empty-pack behavior; and preferred constrained overload selection. \textbf{What was not checked:} KairoMath/Matrix.cppm, KairoECS/SparseSet.cppm or other live external repository code; C++ module build; GPU execution; multithreaded asset lifetime, cancellation, driver synchronization, full exception fault injection, or comparative performance.

\textbf{Language references and research direction:} \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/template_argument_deduction}{cppreference function-template argument deduction}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/constraints}{cppreference constraints and concepts}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/fold}{cppreference fold expressions}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/partial_specialization}{cppreference partial class-template specialization}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/forward}{cppreference \texttt{std::forward}}. Next, benchmark the bounded generic depot against a concrete store and a generational/page-backed alternative using identical payload sizes and publication/failure workloads; do not assume generic dispatch is faster without data.

\textbf{Completion classification:} all 16 original titles mapped to continuous authored teaching and one representative, executable, standards-conscious laboratory. This does not certify 16 independent programs, learner recall, received-source equivalence, cross-platform ABI equivalence, or a current Kairo implementation audit. The next canonical source range is M014, Parts 217--230.

\section{M014 --- Parts 217--230: one tile planner and its compile-time policy decisions}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m014-parts-217230-one-tile-planner-and-its-compile-time-policy-decisions}

\textbf{Status and provenance:} authored reconstruction grounded in the original integrated curriculum and confirmed language references. \textbf{Not a received M014 lesson transcript.} The complete \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#A\%20texture\%20tile\%20that\%20chooses\%20its\%20work\%20before\%20the\%20frame\%20begins}{narrative} continues the same generic asset-pipeline problem from M011--M013: a pixel format and policy may be fixed during compilation while tile dimensions, payload length, failure state, and budget limits still demand runtime validation. Original numbered parts are retained \textbf{only as coverage traceability}, not as a string of disconnected teaching sections.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}

Original part and authoritative title
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
First-use requirement and inspectable witness
