\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
217 · Compile-time type traits & An internal pixel format and empty validation policy need compile-time properties; \texttt{std::is\_empty\_v} and \texttt{std::is\_base\_of\_v} are checked in the lab and semantic limitations are explained. \\
218 · std::integral\_constant and type-level values & \texttt{PixelTraits\textless{}Format\textgreater{}} inherits \texttt{std::integral\_constant\textless{}std::size\_t,Channels\textgreater{}}, giving static channel counts for supported formats. \\
219 · if constexpr and compile-time branching & Known RGBA versus RGB format selects whether a prepared plan has alpha; both variants are tested. \\
220 · constexpr functions & \texttt{checked\_product} performs safe size multiplication both in \texttt{static\_assert} and using runtime inputs; overflow is rejected before multiplication. \\
221 · constexpr objects & \texttt{inline\ constexpr} tile definition and precomputed Morton table are constant-expression objects, distinct from mutable diagnostics. \\
222 · consteval immediate functions & \texttt{make\_tile\_definition} requires a valid compile-time tile configuration, and \texttt{make\_morton\_table} builds the lookup table during constant evaluation. \\
223 · constinit static initialization guarantees & \texttt{constinit\ std::uint32\_t\ g\_plans\_recorded=0} guarantees suitable static initialization but remains mutable and not thread-safe; the single-threaded count is checked. \\
224 · Compile-time lookup tables & A sixteen-entry table spreads four coordinate bits; \texttt{morton4(3,5)=39} is derived and compile-time/runtime tested. \\
225 · Type erasure versus templates & A concrete CRTP recorder and a \texttt{std::function\textless{}void(const\ TilePlan\&)\textgreater{}} target each receive the same plan under different static/runtime contracts; neither is benchmarked. \\
226 · CRTP static polymorphism & \texttt{RecorderBase\textless{}CountingRecorder\textgreater{}::record} calls \texttt{record\_impl} via a derived cast; the derived-object precondition and nonvirtual semantics are explained and tested. \\
227 · Policy-based design & \texttt{TilePlanner\textless{}Format,BudgetPolicy,Recorder\textgreater{}} composes budget validation and recording; both 64-byte bounded and stateless no-budget variants are exercised. \\
228 · Tag dispatch & \texttt{VerifyPayloadTag} and \texttt{MetadataOnlyTag} select overloads that verify real bytes or deliberately produce only a manifest; invalid length is rejected in verified mode. \\
229 · Empty-base optimization & A holder deriving from an empty policy is measured beside an ordinary empty-policy member; observed sizes are toolchain-specific, not promised. \\
230 · \texttt{{[}{[}no\_unique\_address{]}{]}} and zero-size policy storage & A policy member eligible for overlap is used in \texttt{TilePlanner} and an isolated holder; local \texttt{sizeof} results and MSVC portability differences are documented. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Executable witness:} \href{tools/labs/compile_time_asset_policy_lab.cpp}{C++20 reference laboratory}, 258 lines and 10,788 bytes, SHA-256 \texttt{f5eb017280bb6b2ae6b1475dfb014694235f805a6948df36548b17145d889951}, Git blob \texttt{f4379dbce70c261f2832f8311a628e346f7471f7}, byte-identical to the locally executed source. Compiled and executed successfully with GCC 14.2.0 using \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}; Clang 17.0.0 with \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}; and GCC 14.2.0 release-style \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O2\ -DNDEBUG\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}. Each execution printed \texttt{compile\_time\_asset\_policy\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}; local layout observations were \texttt{PlainPolicyHolder=16}, \texttt{EboPolicyHolder=8} and \texttt{CompactPolicyHolder=8} bytes on that Linux target.

\textbf{Evidence limits:} the code plans tiles and checks an optional CPU byte span. It does \textbf{not} allocate native graphics memory, enqueue a GPU transfer, validate a device format, establish a real asynchronous transaction, measure speed, or certify a portable ABI layout. Its \texttt{MetadataOnlyTag} deliberately does not check that a payload exists, so the result cannot be represented as a verified upload. \texttt{constinit} supplies no synchronization, CRTP supplies no runtime plugin selection, and local object-size observations cannot be treated as standard-required sizes. No live Kairo/Maveb repository sources were re-inspected. Research and project-code gates remain open.

\textbf{Language-source anchors:} \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/if}{cppreference \texttt{if\ constexpr}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/constexpr}{\texttt{constexpr}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/consteval}{\texttt{consteval}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/constinit}{\texttt{constinit}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/ebo}{empty base optimization}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/attributes/no_unique_address}{\texttt{{[}{[}no\_unique\_address{]}{]}}, including implementation notes}, and \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/types}{class-template type traits}. These clarify specific language rules; they do not stand in for a real cross-compiler benchmark.

\textbf{Completion classification:} fourteen original titles mapped to one connected, implementable authored chapter and a representative three-toolchain test. It is \emph{not} fourteen separately certified mastery gates or a received-source integration. Next canonical M015, Parts 231--250, should arise as the in-memory asset store moves beyond its fixed-capacity demonstration into standard container growth, invalidation, storage and lookup choices.

\section{M015 --- Parts 231--250: growing the asset registry without losing identities}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m015-parts-231250-growing-the-asset-registry-without-losing-identities}

\textbf{Source status:} authored reconstruction from the original integrated 180-chunk / 3,000-part curriculum contract. \textbf{No received M015 teaching transcript} is claimed. The single coherent explanation is \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#An\%20asset\%20registry\%20grows\%20while\%20its\%20handles\%20must\%20not\%20change}{the growing asset registry narrative}, directly after M014 and before the existing general API-contract chapter. The numbered rows below preserve exact titles and indicate what mechanism required each concept; they are not the main teaching body.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Original part --- exact authoritative title
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Where the problem forces its use; evidence
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
231 · std::vector internal size/capacity/storage model & A bounded asset depot begins growing; the lab distinguishes live elements from reserved storage and checks contiguous values. \\
232 · std::vector geometric growth & Derive the geometric relocation sum and amortized append cost; the lab observes capacity 2→4 on its local implementation, not a standard-prescribed factor. \\
233 · std::vector iterator and pointer invalidation & Growth invalidates old element pointers/iterators; the lab checks the growth event without accessing stale addresses. \\
234 · std::vector reserve versus resize & \texttt{reserve(2)} does not create elements; \texttt{resize(5)} constructs new ones with the tested default \texttt{int} values. \\
235 · std::array fixed contiguous storage & Compile-time fixed channel/counter sequences keep their own element storage and do not use vector growth semantics; array indexing tested. \\
236 · std::deque segmented storage & Editor arrival/consumption needs front/back operations without pretending an entire deque is a single contiguous allocation; front/back/index test. \\
237 · std::list node-based storage & Editing work transfers nodes while retaining iterators to unaffected nodes; \texttt{splice} tested. \\
238 · std::forward\_list singly linked storage & A singly linked worklist removes a successor using \texttt{erase\_after}, needing the predecessor iterator; tested. \\
239 · std::map balanced-tree semantics & Ordered asset names with logarithmic comparison-based operations and ordered iteration; tree shape remains an implementation detail. \\
240 · std::set ordered unique-key semantics & A set of IDs removes duplicates and preserves comparison order; 8,3,8,5 yields three unique values. \\
241 · std::unordered\_map hash-table semantics & Persistent name-to-generation-handle index uses owned \texttt{std::string} keys; its hash lookup and rehash rules are explained. \\
242 · std::unordered\_set hash-table semantics & Pending-name membership without mapped values; duplicate insertion and \texttt{contains} tested. \\
243 · Hash function quality and collision behavior & A deliberately constant hash places all 24 distinct names in one bucket without corrupting key equality; lookup correctness is tested. \\
244 · Load factor and hash-table rehashing & \texttt{load\_factor=n/b}, \texttt{max\_load\_factor(0.5)} and \texttt{reserve(96)} illustrate the bucket policy; old rehash-invalidated iterators are never dereferenced. \\
245 · std::priority\_queue heap-backed abstraction & Uploads require priority selection, plus explicit sequence-number tie-breaking because the adapter alone does not guarantee stability. \\
246 · std::stack adapter semantics & Editor undo/traversal needs LIFO; push 7,8 then pop yields 8 before 7. \\
247 · std::queue adapter semantics & Incoming jobs need FIFO; push 7,8 then pop yields 7 before 8. \\
248 · std::span as a non-owning contiguous view & A checksum consumes byte data synchronously without copying it, with owner lifetime and reallocation constraints explicit. \\
249 · std::string storage and small-string optimization concept & Asset names own characters; locally observed short/long capacities 15/1000 are not portable SSO or allocation guarantees. \\
250 · std::string\_view lifetime rules & Borrowed path substring \texttt{"stone"} is copied into an owning string before the source is changed; substring may lack a terminating NUL. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Tested implementation:} \href{tools/labs/asset_container_lab.cpp}{C++20 reference laboratory}, 292 lines / 13,525 bytes; Git blob \texttt{c9a323bcf4a3f2e41df13db9d10d381bdd71468f}, SHA-256 \texttt{e829cf731b17419e282775cf0b8224c210819ed3d81da36fce45014a04e4afae}. The committed bytes are identical to the local file compiled and executed with GCC 14.2.0 using \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}, Clang 17.0.0 using \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O1\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}, and GCC 14.2.0 using \texttt{-std=c++20\ -O2\ -DNDEBUG\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}. All three executions reported \texttt{asset\_container\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}. The implementation tests two distinct rollback injections (before preparation, after candidate slot creation), stale-handle rejection after slot recycling, list/deque/forward\_list behaviors, pathological hash collisions, load-factor checks, scheduling semantics and safe ownership boundaries.

\textbf{Precisely bounded claims:} \texttt{AssetRegistry} is a single-threaded teaching model. It does not prove correct concurrent deletion, GPU fence retirement, persistent identity across sessions, byte-for-byte stable ABI, all custom allocator/hash exception behaviors, uniformly fast worst-case hashing, or measured performance of competing containers. The example is stronger than an append-only depot---it supports removal and generational reuse---but intentionally lacks the full production allocator/threading/hot-reload model. Project-specific \texttt{KairoECS/SparseSet.cppm} and \texttt{KairoRenderer/RenderGraph.cppm} remain targets \textbf{to verify} from current independent repository source; nothing here is attributed to those implementations.

\textbf{Conceptual references consulted:} \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/container/vector}{cppreference vector}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/container/vector/reserve}{vector reserve}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/container/vector/resize}{vector resize}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/container/deque}{deque}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/container/unordered_map}{unordered\_map}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/container/span}{span}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/string/basic_string_view}{string\_view}. These are supporting language-library sources; the original supplied curriculum remains the title/coverage authority.

\textbf{Completion classification:} all 20 titles connected to one engineered narrative and one compile-tested illustrative registry, not 20 individually completed mastery gates or a newly received source batch. The next canonical M016 / Parts 251--270 introduces optional/variant/any/expected-like results, algorithms and iterator categories only when the registry's queries, transforms and failure return paths actually need them.

\section{M016 --- Parts 251--270: the asset registry becomes a query and transformation pipeline}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m016-parts-251270-the-asset-registry-becomes-a-query-and-transformation-pipeline}

\textbf{Provenance:} authored reconstruction from the canonical integrated 180-chunk / original 3,000-part contract, not a received M016 lesson transcript. The continuous \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20a\%20living\%20asset\%20registry\%20to\%20reliable\%20queries\%20and\%20transformations}{M016 narrative} begins when the Phase 5 generation-checked registry needs an isolated, owned report, typed lookup failures, sorting, searching, aggregation and removal. Its twenty source identifiers remain an audit contract, not twenty separate main chapters.

Original part / exact title

\midrule\noalign{}

251 · std::optional presence-or-absence semantics & \texttt{find\_optional} returns a value-copied logical handle for \texttt{rock.mesh} and \texttt{nullopt} for an absent name; it cannot explain an invalid input. \\
252 · std::variant visitation & Inspect, Upload and Evict are closed, distinct request shapes; \texttt{std::visit} and an overload set count each command. \\
253 · std::any runtime type erasure & Open-ended diagnostics hold a copied string, reject \texttt{any\_cast\textless{}int\textgreater{}} with \texttt{bad\_any\_cast} and release the value at reset. \\
254 · std::expected-style error/value modeling & A deliberately limited C++20 \texttt{ExpectedLike\textless{}T,E\textgreater{}} distinguishes valid handle, empty name and nonexistent name; it is not a complete C++23 \texttt{std::expected} substitute. \\
255 · std::pair and tuple product types & Pair carries count/error fields together; tuple carries report name/count/version; both are product types, not variant alternatives. \\
256 · Structured bindings & \texttt{auto\ {[}..{]}} copies the pair; \texttt{auto\&\ {[}..{]}} aliases the original and updates its error count. \\
257 · Iterators and iterator categories & C++20 static assertions distinguish input, forward, bidirectional and random-access capabilities across stream, forward\_list, list and deque. \\
258 · Contiguous iterators and pointer-like optimization & Vector iterators are contiguous; deque iterators are random-access but not contiguous; both facts statically checked. \\
259 · Iterator invalidation as an API contract & Owned snapshots avoid live-registry relocation; sort changes positional meanings, erase invalidates affected vector iterators, and no invalidated pointer is deliberately accessed. \\
260 · std::sort introsort concept & A byte/name comparator gives a deterministic sorted snapshot; common introsort-like implementations are explained without claiming the standard mandates one. \\
261 · std::stable\_sort stability guarantee & A descending-priority sort preserves sky.tex before ui.tex and brick.mesh before rock.mesh within equal-priority groups. \\
262 · std::partition and partition-based algorithms & Partition mesh vs nonmesh, validate both sides and recover the boundary with \texttt{partition\_point}; no false sorting/stability claim. \\
263 · std::lower\_bound binary search contract & Sort by the byte key and find first non-less-than-48 position, index one; note forward-iterator increment cost. \\
264 · std::upper\_bound and equal ranges & Upper bound for 48 gives index three, \texttt{equal\_range} returns the matching {[}1,3) interval; a bidirectional heterogeneous comparator is supplied. \\
265 · std::find and linear search cost & One-off name lookup scans an owned name vector without rebuilding an index; compare with the live registry's maintained hash index. \\
266 · std::transform data-parallel expression & Convert owned asset records into byte counts with a \texttt{back\_inserter} to a separate destination; no automatic SIMD/threading claim. \\
267 · std::accumulate and reduction order & Ordered left fold with \texttt{std::size\_t\{0\}} yields the exact small payload total 304; explain why accumulator type matters. \\
268 · std::reduce and relaxed ordering implications & Integer test also yields 304; a binary64 regrouping demonstration produces 3 vs 4, without pretending this library must select one grouping. \\
269 · std::remove erase idiom & \texttt{remove\_if} produces a retained logical prefix but leaves vector size six until \texttt{erase} destroys the removed suffix; final IDs are 1,3,5. \\
270 · std::erase\_if modern container erasure & C++20 \texttt{erase\_if} removes two texture rows from the \textbf{report copy} and separately removes one matching unordered diagnostic entry, without evicting the live registry. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Executable witness:} \href{tools/labs/asset_query_algorithm_lab.cpp}{C++20 reference laboratory}, 316 lines / 15,306 ASCII bytes, Git blob \texttt{0fa0a888b2aa0c348a52307a049bbf38527fcde1}, SHA-256 \texttt{c479bd493444d093f6c34898d4ecdf43938cdbd82de8986a7b1906a5bc374f9c}, compared byte-for-byte to the locally executed file. The following actual local executions all reported \texttt{asset\_query\_algorithm\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}: GCC 14.2.0 C++20 with \texttt{-O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}; Clang 17.0.0 C++20 with \texttt{-O1\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}; and GCC 14.2.0 C++20 with \texttt{-O2\ -DNDEBUG\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror}. No ASan/UBSan diagnostic was reported in the witnessed sanitizer run.

\textbf{Important limitations:} the laboratory uses a stand-alone \textbf{owned query snapshot}; it does not read the live Phase 5 registry concurrently, validate that a saved handle is still current, serialize \texttt{std::any} objects, benchmark sorting/binary search/hash tradeoffs, measure parallel execution or guarantee bitwise reproducible floating-point reductions. It does not run Vulkan/Metal or inspect current project-specific \texttt{KairoSpatial/BVH.cppm} or \texttt{KairoRayTracer/Scene.cppm} implementations. Runtime \texttt{std::reduce} grouping is unspecified, and the two explicit floating-point grouping expressions are a reproducibility warning---not a demand that one particular standard-library implementation differ on that input.

\textbf{Source anchors:} original integrated master curriculum for part ownership and titles; \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/optional}{cppreference \texttt{std::optional}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/variant}{\texttt{std::variant}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/any}{\texttt{std::any}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/utility/expected}{\texttt{std::expected} (C++23)}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/iterator}{C++20 iterator concepts}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/algorithm/lower_bound}{\texttt{lower\_bound}}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/algorithm/reduce}{\texttt{std::reduce}}, and \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/container/unordered_map/erase_if}{C++20 \texttt{erase\_if}}.

\textbf{Completion classification:} all 20 exact titles integrated in a continuous authored chapter with one independently compiled representative lab; not twenty separately certified learner mastery gates, a newly received transcript, exhaustive tests, or a current external engine-code audit. Next canonical M017 / Parts 271--290 naturally extends this query pipeline with lazy ranges, allocators/PMR, modules and stable linkage/ABI boundaries.

\section{M017 --- Parts 271--290: lazy asset queries, allocation lifetimes and compiled API boundaries}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m017-parts-271290-lazy-asset-queries-allocation-lifetimes-and-compiled-api-boundaries}

\textbf{Canonical ownership and source distinction:} the original integrated 180-chunk / 3,000-part curriculum assigns M017 Parts 271--290, exactly the titles below. This new teaching is an \textbf{authored reconstruction}, not a newly received M017 lesson transcript. Its full narrative, \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20copying\%20every\%20asset\%20report\%20to\%20a\%20lazy,\%20allocatable,\%20separately\%20compiled\%20query\%20service}{From copying every asset report to a lazy, allocatable, separately compiled query service}, follows M016's safe asset-report pipeline rather than providing twenty isolated articles. The executable CPU path is \href{tools/labs/asset_ranges_pmr_lab.cpp}{asset\_ranges\_pmr\_lab.cpp}. A separate real C++20 module graph, with source and ordered build script, is under \href{tools/modules/build_module_lab.sh}{tools/modules}.

271 · Range-based for translation model & A \texttt{LiveRows} range uses member \texttt{begin/end} and a custom cursor to skip tombstones; the chapter derives the hidden loop and temporary-lifetime boundary. \\
272 · C++20 ranges views as lazy adaptors & \texttt{views::all\ \textbar{}\ filter\ \textbar{}\ transform} selects four live asset names; counters show no predicate or transform evaluation at adaptor construction. \\
273 · Owning ranges versus non-owning views & An lvalue snapshot is borrowed by a ref view, while a temporary snapshot is moved into an owning view; \texttt{borrowed\_range} concepts distinguish iterator survival from ownership. \\
274 · Projection functions in ranges algorithms & \texttt{ranges::find(rows,\ ...,\ \&Row::name)} looks up the name directly, and \texttt{ranges::sort} uses \texttt{\&Row::bytes} without materializing a separate projected-key container. \\
275 · Custom iterators & \texttt{LiveRowIterator} implements a multi-pass, skipping forward cursor; concepts verify forward but not bidirectional/contiguous capabilities, and tests count five live rows. \\
276 · Custom allocators and allocator-aware containers & \texttt{TrackingAllocator\textless{}T\textgreater{}} with shared statistics supplies \texttt{std::vector} storage; acquisition/deallocation calls balance and actual element construction remains the container's responsibility. \\
277 · polymorphic\_allocator and memory\_resource & A counting \texttt{std::pmr::memory\_resource} intercepts allocations for a PMR vector and PMR unordered map with a runtime-selected provider; resource lifetime is explicit. \\
278 · Monotonic\_buffer\_resource behavior & An aligned initial scratch arena spills upstream, retains blocks after its vector's destruction and releases them on arena teardown; a null-upstream bounded arena rejects exhaustion. \\
279 · Unordered-container custom allocators & \texttt{std::pmr::unordered\_map\textless{}std::pmr::string,size\_t\textgreater{}} routes node/bucket and allocator-aware key storage through the selected resource, without asserting implementation-specific node counts. \\
280 · Function objects versus lambdas in hot code & \texttt{AtLeastBytes} and a threshold-capturing lambda produce equal counts; the chapter identifies the measurements needed before any speed or code-size claim. \\
281 · C++ modules: interface units & \texttt{allmight\_asset\_query.cppm} exports a named module with a small handle, preview value, constant, inline function and ordinary function declaration. \\
282 · C++ modules: implementation units & \texttt{allmight\_asset\_query\_impl.cpp} defines \texttt{make\_preview} as part of the named module without exporting its implementation body. \\
283 · Exported declarations & \texttt{export\ namespace} and \texttt{export\ import\ :rules} expose the intended interface to two independent importers, tested by a linked executable. \\
284 · Module partitions & \texttt{allmight\_asset\_query\_rules.cppm} defines the exported \texttt{:rules} partition, compiled before the primary interface and deliberately re-exported. \\
285 · Header units and interoperability & \texttt{asset\_query\_interop.hpp} is explicitly compiled with GCC's \texttt{-x\ c++-user-header} before a header-unit importing translation unit; a separate textual C header is also exercised. \\
286 · Modules versus textual headers & The narrative compares import graphs/CMIs and module fragments with textual \texttt{\#include} and macro/build semantics; the tested project contains both. \\
287 · Linkage inside modules & Named-module attachment and export reachability are distinguished from the separate \texttt{extern\ "C"} wrapper's C language linkage; ordinary C and C++ consumers are linked. \\
288 · Inline functions and ODR across modules & \texttt{inline\ constexpr\ kApiEpoch} and \texttt{inline\ constexpr\ has\_generation} are imported into two separate translation units and link in the same program; nuanced ODR/module caveats remain explicit. \\
289 · Namespaces for engine API organization & Public declarations use \texttt{allmight::asset\_query}; the chapter contrasts source-level naming with module reachability and ABI boundaries. \\
290 · ABI stability versus template-heavy APIs & A versioned, simple C11-compatible wrapper \texttt{allmight\_asset\_approved\_v1(uint64\_t)} is compiled and called by C11; the test does not claim universal cross-toolchain ABI compatibility. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Local execution and exact scope:} \texttt{tools/labs/asset\_ranges\_pmr\_lab.cpp}, 281 lines / 12,243 bytes, Git blob \texttt{8b6984bfcc6111dd5743d9e5b416d3e24c22ec5e}, SHA-256 \texttt{a1c76c44bc377537c732f5c46f771fe0bd2d78fbc7bdbcbd4707573716cdfec7}, byte-identical to locally compiled/tested source. It passed GCC 14.2.0 with C++20, \texttt{-O1\ -g\ -Wall\ -Wextra\ -Wpedantic\ -Werror\ -fsanitize=address,undefined\ -fno-omit-frame-pointer}; Clang 17 with C++20 strict warnings; and GCC release \texttt{-O2\ -DNDEBUG}. Each execution printed \texttt{asset\_ranges\_pmr\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}.

The module graph and script in \texttt{tools/modules/} additionally passed a \emph{local} GCC 14.2.0 \texttt{-std=c++20\ -fmodules-ts} ordered build: rules partition, primary interface, non-exported implementation, two importers, versioned C wrapper, separately compiled C11 consumer and explicitly compiled header unit. Executions printed \texttt{asset\_query\_module\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed}, \texttt{asset\_query\_c\_consumer:\ all\ checks\ passed}, \texttt{asset\_query\_header\_unit\_lab:\ all\ checks\ passed} and the script's final success. GCC's incomplete modules feature support and implementation-specific CMI/build rules remain documented. A separate hosted CI job is configured to exercise the committed module tree; its exact result must be checked rather than inferred from local success.

\textbf{Limits:} these tests prove the modeled CPU view and allocator behavior and the selected GCC module linkage graph; not general cross-compiler module interoperability, GPU ownership, thread safety, allocator performance, portable struct ABI, stable plugin loading, exhaustive error injection or review of the real Kairo source. Original project anchors \texttt{KairoGameEngine/CMakeLists.txt} and \texttt{KairoMath/KairoMath.cppm} remain \textbf{uninspected here}; the new self-contained module example is not an alleged patch to them. Existing original-source gaps and video/book extraction backlogs are preserved.

\textbf{Source/reference bridges:} \href{https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc-14.3.0/gcc/C_002b_002b-Modules.html}{GCC 14 modules documentation} for explicit \texttt{-fmodules-ts}, CMI order and header units; \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/ranges}{C++ ranges} for lazy adaptors and ownership; \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/ranges/owning_view}{owning\_view} for storage and move-only view behavior; \href{https://en.cppreference.com/cpp/memory/monotonic_buffer_resource}{monotonic\_buffer\_resource} for bulk release and thread limits; \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/memory/polymorphic_allocator}{std::pmr::polymorphic\_allocator}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/memory/allocator_traits}{allocator\_traits}, \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/modules}{C++ named modules} and \href{https://en.cppreference.com/w/cpp/language/definition}{One Definition Rule} for source-language contracts. Each reference supports a limited semantic assertion, not an invented performance benchmark.

\textbf{Completion classification:} twenty exact source titles now integrated in a continuous authored lesson; one representative CPU compiler/sanitizer-tested lab; one genuinely compiled GCC14 module/header-unit/C ABI build graph. These are \textbf{not} twenty separately certified learner mastery gates, received M017 transcripts or audits of all mentioned external engine sources. M018 / Parts 291--300 emerges from the need for further ABI isolation, strong math/handle types, and measured abstraction costs at these already established boundaries.

\section{M018 --- Parts 291--300: the same asset service becomes a typed, hidden and measurable engine API}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m018-parts-291300-the-same-asset-service-becomes-a-typed-hidden-and-measurable-engine-api}

\textbf{Canonical boundary:} original curriculum Parts 291--300 are taught in one additive continuous chapter at \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#A\%20registry\%20crosses\%20its\%20last\%20C++\%20boundary:\%20hidden\%20implementation,\%20typed\%20identity\%20and\%20measured\%20abstraction\%20cost}{A registry crosses its last C++ boundary}. This is an \textbf{authored reconstruction}, not a newly received transcript, independent learner assessment or Kairo implementation audit. The design continues directly from M017's separately compiled API and M015--M016's generation-aware registry. The actual multi-translation-unit witness lives in \href{tools/m018/m018_lab.cpp}{m018\_lab.cpp}, \href{tools/m018/asset_catalog.hpp}{the public header}, \href{tools/m018/asset_catalog.cpp}{the hidden implementation} and \href{tools/m018/build_m018_lab.sh}{its build script}.

291 · PIMPL for compilation and ABI isolation & Incomplete Impl in the public header; out-of-line destructor and moves after definition; compiler dependency, deep-const caveat, allocation/indirection cost and ABI limitations are developed. \\
292 · Strong typedefs and type-safe handles & Distinct MeshHandle and TextureHandle, logical slot-generation-catalog identity, invalidation after delete/reuse and cross-catalog rejection; compile-time and runtime checks. \\
293 · Phantom/tag types for units and spaces & WorldSpace/ViewSpace and Meters/Seconds distinguish values at compile time without extra tag fields; actual affine/covector transformations and unit algebra are explained rather than faked by casts. \\
294 · Generic math types constrained by scalar concepts & A deliberately restricted floating-point scalar concept controls Vector and Quantity; limitations for dual, interval and fixed-point scalars and numerical laws are explicit. \\
295 · Compile-time dimensions versus dynamic dimensions & Fixed three-dimensional array/vector comparison with a runtime std::span dot product; mismatched runtime extents throw, and views borrow without owning. \\
296 · Expression templates and temporary-elimination concepts & An eager addition is compared with reference-bearing lazy expression nodes; named inner-node lifetime is tested and temporaries, aliasing and reassociation hazards are explained. \\
297 · Intrusive versus non-intrusive generic abstractions & Caller-owned Task with embedded next pointer, linked-state validation, pop/unlink check and contrast to owning forward\_list nodes; no speed claim. \\
298 · Designing a zero-cost abstraction and verifying its cost & Distinguishes compile-time-only tags from PIMPL's real costs; requires workload, compiler, ISA, allocation count, disassembly and statistically sound benchmark comparisons. \\
299 · Reading generated assembly for a generic C++ function & A separate generic\_sum3 wrapper is compiled to GCC and Clang assembly; no instruction-sequence universality or cycle-count inference is asserted. \\
300 · Building a reusable engine container API with explicit ownership and invalidation rules & Catalog owns Impl and slot storage, rejects stale/foreign handles, returns owned copies, prepares free-list failure safety, and makes moved-from behavior explicit. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Evidence distinction:} the published repository source is real source code, not proof by commentary. Its build script runs GCC release with -DNDEBUG, Clang strict and GCC address/undefined sanitizers on the separately compiled catalog and tests; the tests use a throwing check function rather than assert(), so release checks remain active. It also produces separate optimized assembly for two compilers. Hosted GitHub Actions must be checked for a final passing result; configuring a workflow is not itself evidence that the work ran.

\textbf{Out of scope and remaining hard requirements:} no current KairoMath/Transform.cppm or KairoRenderer/RendererTypes.cppm audit, no full stable binary plugin ABI, no thread-safe catalog, no GPU deferred destruction, no process-persistent handle identity, no exhaustive generation-overflow test or exception-injection matrix, no general expression-template lifetime framework or actual cache/performance benchmark. Parts 301--400 can now build directly on the stated machine-observation method. Prior source gaps, mathematics corpus and video verification queue remain unchanged.

\section{M001 - Parts 1-20}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m001---parts-1-20}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and concrete reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
1 & \hyperref[appendix-ea798b1ceb35]{Physical and logical binary states}: a device maps ranges of a physical measurement to 0/1; thresholds and noise margins let imperfect signals represent stable logical values. Storage retains state, while combinational logic calculates from present inputs. \\
2 & \hyperref[appendix-83c3f28f7bda]{Positional notation}: bit \(b_i\) at position \(i\) contributes \(b_i2^i\). \(N\) independent bits provide \(2^N\) patterns, whose unsigned values run from 0 through \(2^N-1\). \\
3 & \hyperref[appendix-dc552784a2c0]{Binary conversion} by Euclidean division: \(n=2q+r\), \(r\in\{0,1\}\). Save \(r\), repeat on \(q\), and reverse the saved sequence. For 13, remainders 1,0,1,1 give 1101. Handle zero explicitly. \\
4 & Weighted decoding sums set-bit weights. Horner decoding repeatedly uses \(v\leftarrow2v+b\) from left to right. Reading 1101 yields 1,3,6,13, confirming the same value. \\
5 & \hyperref[appendix-6b8075763724]{Hexadecimal} has 16 digits; four bits make one nibble and one hex digit. 1010 is A, 1101 is D, so 10101101 is AD. \\
6 & \hyperref[appendix-17fc1bc8b305]{Octal} has eight digits and groups three bits. Left-pad 10101101 to 010 101 101, giving octal 255. Leading zero in a C++ integer literal requests octal. \\
7 & \hyperref[appendix-5bee2df85484]{Byte and octet}: octet means eight bits; C++ byte width is CHAR\_BIT. \hyperref[appendix-d67a19507dc5]{Machine word} describes a processing convention, not a fixed unit. Exact format widths need explicit types. \\
8 & \hyperref[appendix-dbe5905163c2]{Bit mask}: a pattern selects owned positions. A low-width mask is \(2^w-1\) because subtracting one from a power of two fills all lower positions with ones. \\
9 & \hyperref[appendix-68b52453737c]{Bitwise AND} filters: \(x\land0=0\), \(x\land1=x\). \texttt{AD\ \&\ 07\ =\ 05}. Compare the result with zero for any selected flag, or with the full mask for all selected flags. \\
10 & \hyperref[appendix-a427ce8ce8a6]{Bitwise OR} sets: \(x\lor1=1\), \(x\lor0=x\). \texttt{AD\ \textbackslash{}\textbar{}\ 40\ =\ ED}. Repeating the same set operation has no further effect. \\
11 & \hyperref[appendix-d49161831368]{Bitwise XOR} toggles: \(x\oplus1=\neg x\), \(x\oplus0=x\). \texttt{AD\ \^{}\ 08\ =\ A5}; toggling again restores AD. XOR of two states marks differing positions. \\
12 & \hyperref[appendix-8f38927cfc54]{Bitwise NOT} inverts all positions at a stated width. Eight-bit NOT(0F)=F0; promoted int-width NOT has more positions. State width before interpreting its value. \\
13 & \hyperref[appendix-73425b25118e]{Shift operations} relocate bits. Logical right shift zero-fills; arithmetic right shift sign-fills. Left shift zero-fills low positions; discard behavior and numeric meaning depend on width and language rules. \\
14 & For nonnegative unsigned arithmetic, left shift by \(k\) resembles multiplication by \(2^k\) and right shift floor division. Truncation changes the exact answer; signed division toward zero differs from arithmetic right shift for negative odd values. \\
15 & \hyperref[appendix-ac697f5c66e8]{Bit field} extraction normalizes with right shift and isolates with a low mask: \texttt{(AD\ \textgreater{}\textgreater{}\ 4)\ \&\ 03\ =\ 2}. Mask-only gives 0x20, still at its original significance. \\
16 & Insertion clears the destination mask, shifts the valid new value, then ORs it into the cleared word. Preserve outside-mask bits. Replacing quality 2 with 1 in AD produces 9D. \\
17 & \hyperref[appendix-1f9963e1a5d1]{Bit packing} combines disjoint, range-checked fields. OR contributions 05,08,20,00,80 to obtain AD. Packing saves payload bytes but introduces extraction and update work. \\
18 & Unpacking reverses the declared schema. For LSB-first bits use byte \(\lfloor i/8\rfloor\) and offset \(i\bmod8\). Check the requested count, source capacity, and unused padding before indexing. \\
19 & \hyperref[appendix-24475e1596a8]{Boolean algebra} defines false/true operations by truth tables. \hyperref[appendix-fe6b457d68d8]{Logical versus bitwise operators} differ in result domain; built-in logical AND/OR also \hyperref[appendix-2e245a6fa8d3]{skip unnecessary operands}. \\
20 & \hyperref[appendix-9079d3659da4]{De Morgan\textquotesingle s laws} swap AND and OR when negation crosses the operator. Enumerate four inputs to prove both identities; keep a fixed width for the bitwise versions. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{M002 - Parts 21-40}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m002---parts-21-40}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and concrete reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
21 & Unsigned \(N\)-bit range is \([0,2^N-1]\). Distinguish state count 256 from maximum eight-bit value 255. \\
22 & \hyperref[appendix-ce20506c8532]{Signed magnitude} uses one sign bit and \(N-1\) magnitude bits. It has two zeros and requires sign-aware magnitude arithmetic. Eight-bit −5 is 10000101. \\
23 & \hyperref[appendix-03c678cd40b3]{One\textquotesingle s complement} encodes negatives by bit inversion. −5 is 11111010. All ones is negative zero; addition returns a final carry to the low end as end-around carry. \\
24 & \hyperref[appendix-4fbc03d20829]{Two\textquotesingle s complement} uses the negative residue \(2^N-x\) and signed top-bit weight \(-2^{N-1}\). Decode AD as 173−256=−83. The same adder supports positive and negative residues. \\
25 & Negation follows \(2^N-x=(2^N-1-x)+1\). The first term is the width-limited complement, so −13 is NOT(00001101)+1=11110011. \\
26 & Negating zero gives all ones plus one, leaving low bits zero. Two's complement has exactly one zero and assigns every pattern a distinct signed integer. \\
27 & \hyperref[appendix-05f39b10364d]{Minimum signed value} follows the asymmetric range \([-2^{N-1},2^{N-1}-1]\). For int minimum, same-type negation, abs, or division by −1 cannot represent the positive result. Narrow storage may promote first. \\
28 & \hyperref[appendix-c99977d88b33]{Unsigned modular arithmetic} retains residues modulo \(2^N\). 250+10 becomes 4 when narrowed to eight bits; on the usual host the original byte addition expression is int 260. \\
29 & \hyperref[appendix-1a599c924a4e]{Signed integer overflow} is \hyperref[appendix-f558f5b4d8f2]{Undefined behavior} at the operation type's width. Check bounds before evaluating, or use a type proved large enough. Evaluating overflow and checking afterward is too late. \\
30 & Unsigned 0−1 wraps to maximum; going below signed minimum is signed overflow. A size\_t loop condition \texttt{i\textgreater{}=0} never becomes false. Prefer reverse iteration or a decrement-before-index pattern with a correct termination guard. \\
31 & \hyperref[appendix-d52d5f78ffd1]{Carry and borrow} move arithmetic information between digit positions. Four-bit 13+7 gives low 4 with carry 1. Unsigned carry differs from signed range failure. Borrow conventions depend on architecture. \\
32 & \hyperref[appendix-1652d27a8e90]{Half adder}: sum=A XOR B, carry=A AND B. \hyperref[appendix-d1a5b1e520c2]{Full adder} adds carry-in; sum is three-way parity and carry is the majority of three inputs. A majority function is true when at least two inputs are true. \\
33 & \hyperref[appendix-17917d9fdbe3]{Ripple-carry addition} chains full adders. Worst-case circuit delay grows with width; fixed-width software addition remains constant work. Generate/propagate terms permit \hyperref[appendix-b5c46b2d94ff]{parallel carry networks}. \\
34 & \hyperref[appendix-1c7df340573e]{Subtraction through addition} uses \(A+\operatorname{NOT}_N(B)+1\). Binary 6502-style SBC instead includes C: \(A-B-(1-C)\), so C=1 means no borrow. \\
35 & \hyperref[appendix-a65290d66ed9]{Shift-and-add multiplication} sums one shifted multiplicand for every set multiplier bit. 13×11=13+26+104=143. Both the shifting partial value and accumulator must hold the required wider range. \\
36 & \hyperref[appendix-89985e6a2685]{Binary long division} shifts dividend bits into a partial remainder, then compares and conditionally subtracts the divisor. Check \(D=Qd+R\) and \(0\le R<d\). For 13/3, Q=4,R=1. Zero division and int-min/−1 are invalid. \\
37 & \hyperref[appendix-3999c6209649]{Saturating arithmetic} clamps a widened answer to endpoints: 250+20 becomes 255. Signed saturation is not associative, so evaluation order matters. Do not prematurely clamp an HDR intermediate. \\
38 & \hyperref[appendix-cc04044b5782]{Fixed-width integer types} define exact widths when available. Least and fast families promise at least the width, with different goals. size\_t is for sizes, ptrdiff\_t for differences; plain char signedness varies. \\
39 & \hyperref[appendix-45303d03796a]{Integral promotion} occurs first; \hyperref[appendix-5e2d663adf7f]{Usual arithmetic conversions} then select a common type. Track stored type, promoted type, operation type, and destination conversion separately. \texttt{\textasciitilde{}uint8\_t\{0\}} is usually int −1. \\
40 & \hyperref[appendix-736a2345de4a]{Mixed signedness} can convert negative operands to large unsigned values. Use consistent semantic types or std::cmp\_* helpers; valid indexing still requires nonnegative and less-than-size conditions. std::in\_range checks conversion representability. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\section{Notation and validity}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-notation-and-validity}

\(N\) is carrier width, \(i\) a bit position, \(w\) field width, \(s\) offset, and \(b_i\) a 0/1 digit. \(\land\), \(\lor\), \(\oplus\), and \(\neg\) mean AND, OR, XOR, and NOT; integer vectors require a stated width. \(\ll\) and \(\gg\) denote shifts. Modulo records a residue; floor rounds downward. A bijection pairs every element of one set with exactly one element of the other. An invariant is a property that must remain true across steps. An ALU is an arithmetic and logic unit.

Keep number semantics separate from executable C++ operators. A word-level formula assumes a fixed unsigned carrier; C++ built-in operators act after promotion. For C++23, signed right shift rounds toward negative infinity. Claims about signed overflowing addition remain subject to undefined behavior.

\section{Why these dependencies matter}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-why-these-dependencies-matter}

Representation supplies the weights needed for masks. Boolean operators supply the gates needed for adders. Masks and shifts supply the field operations needed for compact data. Finite width supplies the modular model needed for two's complement. Promotions and conversions determine the domain in which C++ actually performs each operation. See \href{../02-Foundations-II/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-4ad47bdd269a}{Dependency Map (02-Foundations-II, appendix-4ad47bdd269a)} and each concept's prerequisites before reconstructing a later mechanism.

\section{M003 - Parts 41-60}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-m003---parts-41-60}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Part
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Definition, mechanism, and concrete reconstruction cue
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
41 & \hyperref[appendix-bc07c3d25b18]{Endianness} orders significance bytes at increasing addresses. It does not change the integer value or reverse bits inside each octet. Separate significance, address traversal, and intra-byte packing order. \\
42 & \hyperref[appendix-367b6abf460e]{Little endian} puts the least-significant byte first: 0x12345678 produces 78 56 34 12. Extract byte i by unsigned right shift by 8i, then narrow to eight bits; reconstruct by shifting each byte into its significance position. \\
43 & \hyperref[appendix-92e040df969a]{Big endian} puts the most-significant byte first: 12 34 56 78. Read left-to-right with value←256×value+byte: M001 Horner decoding in base 256. \\
44 & \hyperref[appendix-11ba36693aa2]{Byte swapping and network order}: swapping reverses octet significance; host/network conversion is a no-op when orders already match. Classic Internet fields often use big endian, but each protocol defines its own contract. \hyperref[appendix-91d09ebed979]{Serialization} fixes widths/order and validates lengths without native padding. \\
45 & \hyperref[appendix-a1f7c53b4742]{Alignment and padding}: alignment A restricts starting addresses to multiples of A. C++ alignof reports the requirement; alignas requests a supported stronger alignment. Align-up needs nonzero A and overflow checks; an aligned numeric offset is not automatically a live pointer. \\
46 & \hyperref[appendix-f52f8c5a3afd]{Internal and tail padding}: internal padding precedes a member to meet its alignment. Under 1/4/1 member alignments, byte--uint32--byte uses offsets 0/4/8, with three internal padding bytes. \hyperref[appendix-c4ac6bb0fff8]{ABI and object layout} determines actual offsets. \\
47 & Tail padding rounds complete-object size for aligned array stride. The same example ends at byte nine and rounds to size 12; the next object's four-aligned member remains aligned. Never infer sizeof solely by summing payload sizes. \\
48 & \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-5522ad176ca0}{Field reordering and AoS versus SoA (05-Engine-I, appendix-5522ad176ca0)}: order by decreasing alignment can reduce holes. A 1/8/1/4 example commonly shrinks 24→16 bytes. Measure sizeof/alignof/offsetof; ABI compatibility and hot/cold access patterns can outweigh smaller size. AoS stores complete records; SoA has one array per field. \\
49 & \hyperref[appendix-0682dbfc5f0e]{Packed structures and misaligned access}: packing extensions can remove holes but weaken member alignment. They do not define endianness, versions, or lifetime. A packed native object differs from a specified wire format. \\
50 & Misalignment can cross cache-line/page boundaries, slow access, or fault depending on instruction/platform. C++ access still needs a suitably aligned live object. Reconstruct bytes or copy representation into an existing aligned trivially copyable object; copying alone does not normalize endianness. \\
51 & \hyperref[appendix-6af5921bc853]{Pointer arithmetic and provenance}: address is a location identifier; a C++ pointer is a constrained typed access path. Within an array, p+i advances i×sizeof(T) bytes; one-past can be formed but not dereferenced. Numeric addresses alone do not establish access rights. \\
52 & \hyperref[appendix-18ee4f913c3c]{Addresses and virtual memory} translates process-visible addresses to physical addresses. A contiguous vector buffer can span scattered frames. \hyperref[appendix-f897c1ddac8f]{Virtual and physical contiguity} distinguishes address-space adjacency from backing placement. \\
53 & \hyperref[appendix-c2a9ad1e2b49]{Process isolation and page permissions}: each address space has its own mapping context. VA 0x400000 can map to frame 27 in A and frame 913 in B. Shared mappings may select the same frame; read/write/execute/user permissions constrain access. \\
54 & \hyperref[appendix-0c67cd5bf30a]{Pages and page offsets}: a virtual page is an address block; a frame is corresponding physical storage. For P=2\^{}k, VPN=floor(VA/P), offset=VA mod P. The 4-KiB example uses k=12; actual page size depends on platform/configuration, not universally 4096. \\
55 & \hyperref[appendix-01e5905165c4]{Page tables and MMU}: entries supply frame identity, permissions, and state; MMU is the translation/protection mechanism. The \hyperref[appendix-7a2d9c5ce511]{TLB} caches translations, not data. A miss may resolve through page-table walking without a page fault. \\
56 & \hyperref[appendix-cc0909a7ef14]{Page faults and demand paging}: OS intervention is needed under current mapping state. Valid demand loading/zero-fill may install a frame and retry; \hyperref[appendix-7a06e5baac04]{Copy-on-write} may copy on first write. Unmapped/forbidden access may fail. A fault does not necessarily imply disk I/O. \\
57 & \hyperref[appendix-b081b7abe193]{Call stack and stack frames}: calls conventionally use LIFO frames for return state, registers, spills, and some locals. Downward growth is a convention; not every local has a stack slot. Returning a dead-local pointer violates \href{../05-Engine-I/main.pdf\#nameddest=appendix-0b104bcba06b}{Storage duration and object lifetime (05-Engine-I, appendix-0b104bcba06b)}, even if bytes remain. \\
58 & \hyperref[appendix-c3480f7d9ff3]{Dynamic allocation and heap} subdivides OS regions into variable-lifetime blocks. \hyperref[appendix-a7c50686d81f]{Allocator fragmentation} distinguishes slack within allocated blocks (37 requested in a 48-byte class leaves 11) from separated free holes unable to satisfy one request. Allocation cost differs from access latency. \\
59 & \hyperref[appendix-17c93c4c2f19]{Static storage and initialization}: namespace/static objects have program-duration storage. Initialized writable data commonly uses data-like regions; zero-initialized storage uses BSS-like zero-fill without literal executable zero payload. Dynamic initialization and cross-unit ordering need later treatment. \\
60 & \hyperref[appendix-5f9e5e2f0a5e]{Read-only data and code}: text commonly contains instructions, read-only regions constants. Writable-or-executable policy limits writable executable mappings. Modifying a string literal is undefined behavior; const automatic objects are not forced into read-only segments. Segment names/layout are implementation choices. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Bridges and distinctions that must not be omitted}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-bridges-and-distinctions-that-must-not-be-omitted}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
\item
\item
\item
  \hyperref[appendix-78cb248c8355]{ASLR} randomizes mapping locations; a textbook process diagram is not a layout guarantee.
\item
\item
  Thread storage duration exists alongside automatic, static, and dynamic duration; thread-local storage is not simply the call stack. Storage duration governs available storage; lifetime governs the live object within it.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Clarifications:} the layout examples assume stated ABI rules; reordering may break a published ABI. Bounds and lifetime remain necessary even with valid mappings/alignment. Wording cross-checked against the C++ draft \href{https://eel.is/c++draft/basic.align}{alignment}, \href{https://eel.is/c++draft/basic.stc}{storage duration}, and \href{https://eel.is/c++draft/bit.endian}{endianness} sections.

61 & \hyperref[appendix-343615f49ddf]{IEEE-754 floating point}: finite nonuniform representation plus explicitly specified rounding/special-value behavior; formats do not make every C++ float binary32. \\
62 & \hyperref[appendix-5057f9ad327f]{Binary32}: 1/8/23 stored fields, 24-bit normal precision and bias 127; 1.0 has word 3F800000. \\
63 & \hyperref[appendix-5078dfab64e6]{Binary64}: 1/11/52 stored fields, 53-bit normal precision and bias 1023; more precision is distinct from more exponent range. \\
64 & \hyperref[appendix-3a048f032c1f]{Signed zero}: floating sign is separate from magnitude; signbit sees negative zero whereas x\textless0 does not. \\
65 & \hyperref[appendix-2fae058c82e1]{Exponent bias}: normal stored exponent E=e+B; E=130 decodes to e=3 for binary32; classify reserved fields first. \\
66 & \hyperref[appendix-a24fd710cf67]{Significand and hidden bit}: normal leading binary 1 is implicit; 23 fraction bits plus it give 24 significant bits. \\
67 & \hyperref[appendix-6854d0e2218e]{Floating-point encoding and decoding}: 10.625=1.010101₂×2³ gives S=0,E=130,F=2A0000, word 412A0000. \\
68 & \hyperref[appendix-6854d0e2218e]{Floating-point encoding and decoding}: C1540000 has S=1,E=130,F=540000; reconstruct −1.65625×8=−13.25. \\
69 & \hyperref[appendix-3a048f032c1f]{Signed zero}: 00000000 and 80000000 compare numerically equal but have distinct representations/reciprocals. \\
70 & \hyperref[appendix-eb336149c00a]{Floating-point infinities}: all-one exponent with zero fraction; distinct from maximum finite and dependent on sign/rounding in overflow. \\
71 & \hyperref[appendix-d6607cc4c235]{NaNs and payloads}: all-one exponent with nonzero fraction; unordered comparisons, quiet/signaling distinction, payload preservation caveats. \\
72 & \hyperref[appendix-00a6e044c437]{Subnormals and gradual underflow}: E=0 uses no hidden one; binary32 x=(-1)\^{}S F×2\^{}-149, filling the normal-to-zero gap. \\
73 & \hyperref[appendix-390ea641da54]{Machine epsilon}: spacing above 1, 2\^{}-23 for binary32, not min(), denorm\_min(), or a universal tolerance. \\
74 & \hyperref[appendix-14bab4cab9d4]{ULP and representable spacing}: normal binade spacing is 2\^{}(e-23) for binary32; 1 has different immediate lower/upper gaps. \\
75 & \hyperref[appendix-614afe1de592]{Rounding to nearest ties to even}: choose nearest neighbor; exact halfway picks even retained significand; guard/round/sticky summarize discarded bits. \\
76 & \hyperref[appendix-637d164ae5ca]{Directed rounding modes}: upward, downward and toward-zero are signed directions; runtime environment changes must be checked and restored. \\
\end{longtable}
}

77 & \hyperref[appendix-2a2f1b74bdd3]{Terminating fractions and dyadic rationals}: a reduced fraction terminates in binary iff its denominator is a power of two; 0.1f stores 13421773/2\^{}27, not 1/10. \\
78 & \hyperref[appendix-a1b1b4580dfd]{Floating-point addition and absorption}: align exponents, preserve guard/round/sticky, normalize and round; 2\^{}24+1 is a nearest-even tie that stores 2\^{}24. \\
79 & \hyperref[appendix-a2773a6a2f9d]{Cancellation and loss of significance}: nearly equal operands expose prior error relative to a small difference; sensitivity factor \(\frac{\lvert a\rvert+\lvert b\rvert}{\lvert a-b\rvert}\). \\
80 & \hyperref[appendix-399b7ad53f39]{Sterbenz lemma}: nearby nonnegative stored floats satisfying x/2≤y≤2x have an exact representable difference under suitable gradual-underflow rules; input uncertainty remains. \\
81 & \hyperref[appendix-ed8531b46754]{Floating-point exception flags}: max×2 overflows; min-normal/2 is exact subnormal, while min-subnormal/2 rounds to zero with tiny/inexact behavior in the tested environment. \\
82 & \hyperref[appendix-3ae74e89b4e6]{Absolute and relative error}: absolute discrepancy carries units; relative discrepancy divides by nonzero reference magnitude. Distinguish forward/backward error and stability from conditioning. \\
83 & \hyperref[appendix-0ec599f8ba27]{Numerical tolerance policies}: separate absolute and relative tolerances; validate them and non-finite inputs. Numeric exact equality is not representation equality. \\
84 & \hyperref[appendix-0ec599f8ba27]{Numerical tolerance policies}: a global epsilon mixes units, scales, algorithmic error and conditioning; approximate closeness is nontransitive. \\
85 & \hyperref[appendix-41e8ef22a21e]{Fused multiply-add}: one-rounding fl(ab+c) differs from fl(fl(ab)+c); the (1+2\textsuperscript{-23)}2 witness yields 2\^{}-46 rather than zero. \\
86 & \hyperref[appendix-c2ac4a84c283]{Floating-point reassociation}: (a+b)+c and a+(b+c) can differ; naive, compensated and fixed pairwise reductions have different contracts. \\
87 & \hyperref[appendix-c2ac4a84c283]{Floating-point reassociation}: distributive graphs differ: with a=1e10,b=1+2\^{}-23,c=-1, factored result 1192.0928955078125 versus unfused expanded 1024. \\
88 & \hyperref[appendix-5f1a2c3f97ff]{Numerical reproducibility}: precision, ordering, FMA, environment and compiler/math-library settings matter. Atomicity does not fix global order; capped TSDF updates are mathematically history-dependent. \\
\end{longtable}
}

89 & \hyperref[appendix-1d0dbe8924cc]{Fixed-point representation}: a signed raw integer I at scale S represents I/S; F=16 gives step 2\^{}-16 and raw 212992 for 3.25. \\
90 & \hyperref[appendix-ceb4c4a9b5a1]{Fixed-point range and resolution}: require 2\^{}-F≤requested step and M·2\textsuperscript{F≤2}(N-1)-1. A 32-bit domain ±1e6 with step≤0.001 permits F=10 or 11. \\
91 & \hyperref[appendix-3333db47830f]{Fixed-point rescaling}: multiply at scale S², then round/divide by S; division uses A·S/B. Prove intermediate width, handle negative ties and zero divisors, and check destination range. \\
92 & \hyperref[appendix-2ed044358b82]{Arithmetic policies} remain necessary for fixed point. Nearest unclipped absolute error≤1/(2S); near-zero relative error can be large. Controlled integer arithmetic can improve repeatability, not make every operation associative. \\
93 & \hyperref[appendix-d5da3eaabaef]{Affine quantization and zero point} maps finite values to codes and reconstructs s(q-z). Groupwise scales cost metadata; source-shaped offset INT4 codes {[}1,5,8,11,15{]} pack to 51 B8 0F. One-bit row centroids are not automatically ±1. \\
94 & \hyperref[appendix-b2402c453dac]{UNORM and SNORM}: UNORM8 reconstructs q/255; SNORM8 clamps q/127 at −1. Quantized normal components can require safe renormalization; sRGB is a separate interpretation. \\
95 & \hyperref[appendix-34443cec7170]{Binary16} uses 1/5/10 fields, bias15, 11 normal precision bits, maximum65504, normal minimum2\^{}-14, subnormal minimum2\^{}-24. Word3E00 encodes1.5. \\
96 & \hyperref[appendix-18b8dd966b5d]{Bfloat16} uses1/8/7 fields, bias127, 8 normal precision bits; it trades detail for wide exponent range. Nearest-even conversion must classify NaNs instead of discarding their low payload into infinity. \\
97 & \hyperref[appendix-34ee45f161c1]{Bit casting and representation} transfers suitable initialized object representation; numeric cast converts a value. Eligibility does not solve byte order, arbitrary valid representations, alignment or mapped-object lifetime. \\
98 & \hyperref[appendix-590bbb2424c1]{Binary format contracts} declare widths/order/representation/version; \hyperref[appendix-652d81568de9]{Checked binary parsing} validates products, subtracted bounds, exact lengths and resource limits. Magic/version are not integrity or authenticity. \\
99 & \hyperref[appendix-ed338677de0a]{Checksums CRCs and hashes} summarize specified content. Additive sums miss byte reordering; CRC requires named parameters; a cryptographic digest needs a trusted reference, MAC or signature for an adversarial trust claim. \\
100 & \hyperref[appendix-230b59c0676b]{Memory dump interpretation} decodes by offset/schema/byte order. The 2×3 demonstration has a28-byte header and24-byte binary32 payload; identical bytes have different meanings under integer, float or character interpretations. \\

\section{Phase 8 - Parts 701-800}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-phase-8---parts-701-800}

Seven supplied excerpts cover every numbered lesson from 701 through 800. The attached set includes an unnumbered eigenanalysis-conditioning repair and an SVD geometric bridge; both are integrated into the teaching narrative without being counted as numbered parts. The final supplied excerpt continues through Part 800, even though the request described the current stopping point as Part 770. This ledger follows the complete supplied set. Source letters P8-A through P8-G are provenance labels only.

Standard deviation --- Part 915 & Variance uses squared deviations, so its units are the square of the underlying variable's units. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
916 & 16. Covariance: measuring coupled uncertainty --- Part 916 & Variance describes the spread of one variable. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
917 & 17. Correlation: normalized covariance --- Part 917 & Covariance depends on the units of its variables. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
918 & 18. Joint distributions --- Part 918 & So far, we have often treated random variables one at a time. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
919 & 19. Marginal distributions --- Part 919 & A marginal distribution describes one variable without retaining the detailed value of the other variables. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
920 & 20. Conditional distributions --- Part 920 & A conditional distribution describes one variable after accounting for information about another. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#3.\%20Expected\%20values\%20describe\%20centers;\%20covariance\%20describes\%20dependence/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M055 \\
921 & 21. Bernoulli distribution --- Part 921 & A Bernoulli random variable represents one binary trial. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#4.\%20Discrete\%20and\%20continuous\%20distribution\%20families/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
922 & 22. 923 & 23. Uniform distribution --- Part 923 & The continuous uniform distribution assigns equal probability density across an interval. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#4.\%20Discrete\%20and\%20continuous\%20distribution\%20families/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\

Gaussian or normal distribution --- Part 924 & The Gaussian distribution is one of the most important continuous probability families. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#4.\%20Discrete\%20and\%20continuous\%20distribution\%20families/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
925 & 25. The standard normal distribution --- Part 925 & The standard normal distribution is the special case: & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#4.\%20Discrete\%20and\%20continuous\%20distribution\%20families/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
926 & 26. Exponential distribution --- Part 926 & The exponential distribution models a nonnegative waiting time in a memoryless event process. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#5.\%20Waiting\%20times,\%20event\%20counts,\%20and\%20covariance\%20geometry/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
927 & 27. Poisson distribution --- Part 927 & The Poisson distribution models counts of events under an idealized Poisson process or other situations satisfying the appropriate count law. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#5.\%20Waiting\%20times,\%20event\%20counts,\%20and\%20covariance\%20geometry/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
928 & 28. Multivariate Gaussian distribution --- Part 928 & We now extend the Gaussian distribution from one uncertain scalar to an entire uncertain vector. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#5.\%20Waiting\%20times,\%20event\%20counts,\%20and\%20covariance\%20geometry/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
929 & 29. Covariance matrices in probability --- Part 929 & We have already introduced covariance matrices, but now we will study their full structure. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#5.\%20Waiting\%20times,\%20event\%20counts,\%20and\%20covariance\%20geometry/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T01 · M056 \\
930 & 30. 932 & 2. 934 & 4. Linear congruential generators --- Part 934 & A linear congruential generator, or LCG, uses: & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#6.\%20A\%20computer\%20produces\%20deterministic\%20pseudorandom\%20sequences/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T06 · M057 \\
935 & 5. Mersenne Twister --- Part 935 & The Mersenne Twister family was designed to produce long-period pseudorandom sequences with strong equidistribution properties for its intended applications. & \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#6.\%20A\%20computer\%20produces\%20deterministic\%20pseudorandom\%20sequences/}{Phase 10 explanation} & T06 · M057 \\

937 & 7. 938 & 8. \section{Phase 4 CPU architecture and measured execution --- Parts 301--400}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-phase-4-cpu-architecture-and-measured-execution-parts-301400}

\textbf{Original contract and source distinction:} the exact part titles below are taken from the supplied original 3,000-part curriculum appended to the integrated 180-chunk v2 PDF. M019 is 301--310, M020 311--324, M021 325--346, M022 347--360, M023 361--384, M024 385--400. This is \textbf{authored expansion} rather than receipt of the original standalone phase transcripts or a certificate of mastery.

\textbf{Primary teaching:} \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20a\%20correct\%20C++\%20asset\%20service\%20to\%20the\%20instructions\%20a\%20CPU\%20can\%20execute\%20—\%20Phase\%204,\%20M019–M021,\%20Parts\%20301–346}{CPU instructions, memory locality and SIMD} continues directly into \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20temporary\%20memory\%20to\%20finished\%20parallel\%20frames\%20—\%20Phase\%204,\%20M022–M024,\%20Parts\%20347–400}{allocators, memory models, job systems and profiling}. The \href{tools/labs/phase4_cpu_systems_lab.cpp}{Phase 4 C++20 laboratory} exercises selected invariants and includes an optional, non-gating comparison benchmark.

\textbf{Remaining evidence requirements:} cache associativity and TLB effects require hardware/profile evidence; genuine SIMD acceleration and vectorization claims require disassembly and target ISA; actual Kairo/Maveb code claims require reading their current revisions; work stealing, high-contention lock-free code and GPU deferred destruction are not implemented by this representative lab. The original book/caption source review queue remains unchanged. The table maps \emph{coverage}, not proven reader mastery or universal performance.

\section{Phase 5 algorithms and data-structure workload contracts --- Parts 401--500}\label{appendix-fd090d982a15-phase-5-algorithms-and-data-structure-workload-contracts-parts-401500}

\textbf{Original contract:} the exact original titles below come from the 3,000-part curriculum appended to the supplied integrated 180-chunk plan. The chunk boundaries are M025 401--410, M026 411--430, M027 431--445, M028 446--467, M029 468--486 and M030 487--500. The content is \textbf{new authored reconstruction}, not a newly received original lesson transcript, and the original parts act as coverage atoms rather than as isolated teaching pages.

\textbf{Continuous teaching:} \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#The\%20scene\%20is\%20now\%20too\%20large\%20for\%20the\%20wrong\%20container\%20—\%20Phase\%205,\%20M025–M027,\%20Parts\%20401–445}{From ADTs to arrays, hashing, trees and DSU} connects to \href{Continuous\%20Notes/Computing\%20Foundations\%20-\%20From\%20Bits\%20to\%20C++\%20and\%20Systems\%20APIs\#From\%20an\%20asset\%20map\%20to\%20a\%20correct\%20navigable\%20world\%20—\%20Phase\%205,\%20M028–M030,\%20Parts\%20446–500}{From graphs to paths, sorting, scans and caches}. The \href{tools/labs/phase5_algorithm_workload_lab.cpp}{Phase 5 C++20 lab} checks selected rings, linear probing with tombstones, Bloom, DSU/MST, graph paths/DAGs, scans, Morton keys, LRU, DP and reproducible randomized properties. Trees such as AVL/red-black/B+ and GPU parallel scans remain conceptual until their own implementations are tested.

\textbf{Remaining evidence obligations:} no current KairoECS/KairoSpatial source was audited. No AVL/Red-black/B-tree/radix implementation or formal worst-case test suite was executed. No GPU prefix scan or dynamic-graph shortest-path benchmark is established. The lab's pseudo-random tests are reproducible but not a statistical proof of uniformity, and expected O(1) hashing is conditional on workload/hash assumptions. The full source-video/caption review queue remains open; every claim of ``authored coverage'' is separate from personal mastery, integrated external source coverage and real-engine behavior.

\textbf{Do not misreport full Phase 28 as implemented:} M163--M164 have a limited CPU-only executable reference (selected opcodes and peripheral stand-ins). Existing nonreceived source intervals, 15,475 pending caption/book claims and source verification requirements remain open.
